### On the Philosopher's Stone.

Because in the **governance of the fire** <note>original: "Regiment des Fewers"; the controlled application of heat at specific intensities and durations</note> which draws out and drives the air, the fire cannot cause the **red tincture** <note>The "tincture" is the active, transformative essence of the Stone that can transmute base metals into gold</note> to emerge unless the fire is strengthened by a good deal until it becomes red. Once it is red, pour **Paradise Water** <note>Previously defined as a highly purified distillate or solvent</note> over it until it has drunk up all its water and everything has become a powder. Then govern the fire until the powder becomes white again, just like snow. Then increase the fire until it is perfectly yellow again, and keep it always stronger until it becomes red, and then your work is ready and finished.

You have seen in this work all four elements, each one particularly. First you saw Water and Earth; they were manifest at the beginning, and the Air was from them, which you also saw afterward in the **yellowness** <note>original: "Gielbe," referring to the *citrinitas* stage, a transitional phase between the white and red works</note>. And now you see the Fire in its redness, as it then stands on the outside.

Therefore the Ancients spoke truly when they said that our Stone is made of four elements; yet it was nonetheless no more than one, and from one thing. And they told the truth, as has been related. But if you wish to have only the **White Stone** <note>The White Stone or *albedo* stage is capable of transmuting metals into silver, whereas the Red Stone produces gold</note>, then give it the Paradise Water just as you did with the <term>ferment: a substance added to the alchemical matter to trigger a specific transformation, acting as a catalyst for the "leavening" of the Stone</term>, and let it ascend and descend <note>Referring to the process of distillation and condensation within the sealed glass vessel</note> until it remains lying at the bottom. And then maintain the same governance of the fire until the powder becomes perfectly white again; thus you have the white

# Isaac Hollandus

... its soul to [it], so that it never separates from the body in eternity. Geber <note>Geber is the Latinized name of Jabir ibn Hayyan (c. 721–815), a foundational figure in alchemy whose writings heavily influenced European practitioners</note> says: make a wedding, and lay the bride to bed with her bridegroom, and pour over them both **Heavenly Dew** <note>original: "Himmel Thau"; an allegorical term for a purified distillate or spirit used to moisten the "body" of the stone</note>. And the bride shall conceive a son, who shall be a king over all his race, and all his enemies shall have peace with him, and he shall be crowned with a **red diadem** <note>The red diadem signifies the *rubedo* or "Red Work," the final stage of alchemical perfection producing the stone that turns metals into gold</note>.

After that, they took the materials and put them back into the glass, and they took the **Paradise Water** <note>Likely the same substance as the "Heavenly Dew," a highly refined solvent or mercurial water</note> and poured it over the material—exactly 10 parts of Paradise Water to 1 part of the material. And this is what Geber says: "Moisten the bed with Heavenly Dew and the bride shall conceive a son."

Then they sealed the glass and placed it back into the furnace, and they increased the fire a little until the Paradise Water rose up and fell back down as drops upon the earth. This was the "Son who makes shining," who must be fed with his **milk** <note>Alchemical "milk" refers to the continued addition of liquid nutrients (solvents) to "nourish" and grow the power of the Stone</note> until he is fully grown.

My child <note>The author uses this paternalistic address common in alchemical "testaments" to speak directly to the apprentice/reader</note>, you must know and understand well: if you wish to make the **Red Stone**, you must bring the white earth to redness using its **ferment** <note>An additive, often gold itself, used to "ripen" the stone at the final stage</note> and by strengthening the fire until it turns yellow. When it is perfectly yellow, like **saffron** <note>original: "crocus"; the deep yellow-orange color of the *citrinitas* stage</note>, then the **element of Air** <note>original: "Elementum aeris"</note> has been turned outward. Then the fire shall be noticeably increased, and the **element of Fire** <note>original: "Elementum Ignis"</note> will come out...

<meta>The text cuts off here, with the catchword "denn" (for/then) indicating the explanation of the final fire stage continues on the next page.</meta>

# On the Philosophical Stone <note>original: "De lapide Philosophico"</note>

Because under the **governance of the fire** <note>The "governance" or "regiment" of fire refers to the careful control of heat levels, which determines the success of the chemical reaction</note> which draws and drives out the air, the fire cannot cause the **red tincture** <note>The "tincture" is the concentrated essence or color that grants the Stone its power to transform metals</note> to emerge—unless the fire is strengthened by a good portion until it becomes red. Once it is red, pour **Paradise Water** over it until it has drunk up all its water and everything has become powder. Then govern the fire until the powder becomes white again, like snow; and then increase the fire until it is perfectly yellow again, and ever stronger until it becomes red, and then your **Work** is ready and accomplished.

&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;You have seen in this **Work** all four **Elements**, each one specifically. First you saw Water and Earth; they were first made manifest. And the Air was within them, which you also saw afterwards in the **yellowness** <note>original: "Gielbe"; refers to the *citrinitas* stage of the alchemical process</note>. And now you see the Fire in its **redness** <note>original: "Röthe"; the *rubedo* or final stage</note>, as it then stands on the outside.

&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;Therefore the ancients spoke truly when they said that our **Stone** is made from four elements. And yet it was no more than one, and from one thing; and they spoke the truth, as has been told. But if you only wish to have the **White Stone**, then give it the **Paradise Water**, just as you did with the **ferment** <note>The substance added to "ripen" or trigger the transformation of the base material</note>, and let it rise and descend <note>This describes "circulation," where a liquid is boiled and condensed so it repeatedly washes over the solid matter</note> until it remains lying at the bottom. And then maintain that same governance of the fire until the powder becomes perfectly white again; thus you have the white [Stone]

Isaaci Hollandus <note>Isaac Hollandus was a legendary 15th-century alchemist whose writings were highly influential in the development of early modern chemistry; he is known for his detailed, practical laboratory instructions.</note>

The Stone is perfect for transmuting all **imperfect bodies** <note>"Imperfect bodies" refers to base metals like lead, tin, or copper, which alchemists believed were "diseased" versions of the perfect metals, gold and silver</note> into **true Silver** <note>original: "veram lunam"; literally "true moon," the alchemical name for silver</note>. Therefore, my child, you should know that all these methods of working are good, and their **projection** <note>"Projection" is the final step of the alchemical process where a small amount of the Stone is cast upon a larger mass of molten base metal to transform it into precious metal</note> is so high that it is wondrous, and you shall find it to be so yourself.

For if you throw your medicine <note>The "medicine" refers to the finished Philosophical Stone in powder or liquid form</note> onto a metal or onto **Mercury** <note>symbol: ☿</note>, for as long as the medicine has power, the metal will remain **brittle** <note>original: "frangibili"</note> like glass, so that it may be ground into powder. Then, some of this powder can be thrown onto another metal, and it will all still be medicine. In this way, one can always throw it onto something else, depending on how high its projection is, whether onto **Gold** <note>symbol: ☉; original: "Sol"</note> or **Silver** <note>symbol: ☽; original: "Luna"</note>, according to how the medicine was prepared.

## Multiplication <note>original: "Multiplicatio"; the process of increasing the quantity or the "potency" of the Philosophical Stone so that the alchemist does not run out of the substance</note>

Now I will teach you how and in what manner you should **multiply** the Stone, so that you do not have to make the Stone more than once in your entire life; after that, you may make as much of it as you wish, even up to 100 pounds and more.

Take 10 or 12 pounds of **Mercury** <note>symbol: ☿</note> and **sublimate** <note>original symbol: ♎ir, for *sublimir*; the process of heating a substance into vapor and collecting the purified solid that forms upon cooling</note> it through Roman Vitriol <note>A sulfate mineral, likely copper sulfate, used as a powerful reagent to purify the mercury</note>, using 3 parts Vitriol and 1 part Mercury. This **sublimation** <note>original symbol: ♎on</note> must be done 6 or 7 times with new and fresh Vitriol. Thus the Mercury is prepared; keep it until you need it. After that, take 1 ounce of fine **Gold** <note>symbol: ☉</note> that has been **cemented** <note>"Cementing" is a purification technique where metal plates are layered with powdered chemicals and heated for a long period to remove impurities</note> in the **Vegal** <note>A specialized alchemical furnace or vessel arrangement frequently mentioned in Hollandus's recipes</note> and melt it in a crucible. Then throw 1 ounce of your medicine into it; let it sit for a short time, as long as it takes to say two **Pater Nosters** <note>The "Our Father" prayer; in the absence of clocks, alchemists used the time it took to recite specific prayers to measure laboratory durations</note>...

# Of Isaac Hollandus <note>original: "Isaaci Hollandi"</note>

...is just as good as the first in a short time.

Furthermore, my child <note>Hollandus, like many alchemists, addresses the reader as "child" or "son" to signify the transmission of secret knowledge from master to pupil</note> should know that there have been some who took 9 parts of **Paradise Water** <term>Paradise Water: a highly purified alchemical solvent or elixir often mentioned in this work</term>, which is already prepared, and 1 part of the **Stone**, and put them into 9 vials <note>original: "violen"</note>, well-luted <term>Luted: sealed with "lute," a specialized airtight clay or cement used to prevent the escape of vapors during heating</term>, and placed them thus on a **tripod** <note>original: "tripodem"; a three-legged stand for heating vessels over a furnace</note> in the innermost chamber until the Stone was ready and until the Paradise Water was to be poured upon it. Afterward, they took one vial out and poured it into the Paradise Water, mixing them well together, and stopped it up again securely with **lute** <note>original: "Lute"</note>, and set it back in its place. The other 8 parts they let stand until a portion of the Stone became **fixed** <note>original: "fix"; in alchemy, to "fix" a substance is to make it solid and resistant to evaporation by heat</note>. Thus they did with all 8 glasses, giving each its requirement until all 9 had "drunk" their proportion and were thereby fixed.

This seems to me to be the better manner, for it shortens the work and proceeds easily, because it is not overloaded as it would be if it all stood together in a single glass. It is indeed somewhat more laborious, but nevertheless better that one should pour from these 9 parts onto one part of the Stone in this way; and thus the Ancients did and taught their children. Understand and hear these words, for they have said: one should take 9 parts of **May-Dew** <note>original: "Meyen-Tawes"; dew collected in the month of May was highly prized by alchemists as it was believed to contain a potent celestial spirit or "spiritus mundi"</note>, each luted <note>original: "lutiret"</note> into a vial and held on a tripod with equal heat until the bottom became white and dry. Then they moistened the bottom well with 1 part [to] 6 parts of **May-Dew**.

### Concerning the Philosophical Stone <note>original: "De lapide Philosophico"</note>

...and then with the other part, and so on until the last part, until it flows like oil.

You should know that the ancients made the Stone in many different manners, and in the end, it was all good. Know that the ancients worked as I have recounted; but their descendants have found many other forms of the work to shorten the Art, such as making it with **Strong Water** <term>Aqua Fortis: Latin for "strong water," refers to nitric acid, a powerful solvent used to dissolve metals</term> in all sorts of ways, as is taught in many places concerning the Mineral work, as well as in the **animal** realm, and even more so in the **vegetable** realm <note>Alchemists divided the natural world into three "kingdoms"—Mineral, Animal, and Vegetable—believing the secret of life and transformation could be found in each</note>, wherein wonders are hidden. There, one finds miraculous waters that seem to be **miracles** <note>original: "Miracula"</note>, as is taught in many places concerning the Vegetable realm. They have also found how to separate the **Elements** <note>the four basic constituents of matter: Earth, Air, Fire, and Water</note> in various manners, as is also taught in the work in some places; this proceeds with great subtlety and care. They have all sought to shorten the time required for the sake of testing Nature, but in that work there is great care, much labor, and great expense and uncertainty. Therefore, I advise you, my dear children, avoid all works where there is a separation of the elements, or the making of many kinds of **Strong Water** <term>Aqua Fortis</term>, as I have taught in the **prologue** <note>original: "prologo"</note> and shall yet teach.

We have taught how our ancestors <note>original: "Eltern," literally "parents," used here to mean alchemical predecessors</note> dealt with **amalgams** <note>original: "amalgammis"; mixtures of mercury with other metals</note>, and they all came to a certain and successful end; and it is so good that it cannot be improved upon.

## Now I will teach how you should make the Stone with simple waters <note>original: "schlechten Wassern"; in this context, "simple" or "plain" waters, likely referring to distilled substances or rainwater rather than the corrosive "Strong Waters" (acids) mentioned previously</note>, and also the *Oils* <note>original: "Olea"</note> from the metals, which shall also proceed without much labor, and without any separation of the Elements, and how to bring them to *perfection* <note>the state of being fully transformed into gold or the medicinal Elixir</note>, which is safe and good.

But the reason why one separates the *Elements* <term>the four building blocks of matter: Earth, Air, Fire, and Water</term> happens for the sake of one thing: so that one makes the *imperfect* *perfect*, and also for the sake of the impurity, to separate it from the substance, so that the *Body* <note>original: "Corpus"; the physical, solid part of the metal</note> and Spirit <note>the volatile or energetic part of the metal</note> thus become stripped of all impurity, and are afterward *conjugated* <note>original: "conjungirt"; meaning to be chemically and spiritually reunited</note> again.

Now know that every thing that is placed in the fire, however impure it may be, the fire makes it clean and pure, as we have previously taught. The first sign is a perfect blackness <note>This is the "Nigredo" or black stage, the first essential step in the Great Work, representing the "death" or decomposition of the matter</note>, and we see that with our eyes—that all *Matter* <note>original: "Materia"</note> becomes as black as pitch. How does that happen? The fire drives the *corruption* <note>original: "corruption" or "Fäule"; the decaying or transitional state of the material</note> to the top, and it leaves the *Matter* through the power of the heat; however, it does not happen with a strong fire, but by a gentle fire. Thus the *corruption* or *dregs* <note>original: "corruptio" or "feces"</note>, which are in the vessel <note>original: "arca"; literally "ark" or "chest," referring here to the sealed alchemical container</note>, are thus sweetly driven upward until the whole is black.

Therefore says *Morienus* <note>a legendary 7th-century alchemist and hermit often cited in medieval texts</note>, see well to it that

<summary>Isaac Hollandus introduces a "simple" method for creating the Philosopher's Stone and metallic oils through purification by fire. He explains that a gentle heat drives out impurities, resulting in the "Nigredo" or black stage, which is the first sign of successful transmutation.</summary>

->### Concerning the Philosopher's Stone.<-

...you must govern your fire so that you do not obtain a **Whiteness** before the **Blackness**, or else the entire work is spoiled. By avoiding Whiteness before Blackness, this must be a secure way for the <term>corruption</term> <note>original: "corruptio"; referring to the impurities or the decaying state of the base matter</note> to be driven out through the fire, and thus the cleansing and <term>perfection</term> <note>the final, perfected state of the Philosopher's Stone</note> must occur. Be cautious in our Work, for after long and constant cooking, the heat consumes the <term>corruption</term>, the <term>dregs</term> <note>original: "feces"; the solid remains or waste matter left after heating</note>, and the **Blackness**, and brings them into another color, and always another, until it becomes <term>perfectly</term> white, like snow. 

And this happens gently, so that the <term>Elements</term> <note>The four building blocks of matter: Fire, Air, Water, and Earth</note> are not forced, but are mildly <term>rectified</term> <note>purified or corrected through the alchemical process</note> of all their impurity. But guard yourself in every way, as <term>Morienus</term> <note>A legendary alchemist, often cited as a teacher of King Khalid; he represents the ancient hermetic tradition</note> warns, that you do not obtain the **Redness** before the **Whiteness**, for one must not burn our Stone during the work. 

And know that this is the best way; for where the separation of the <term>Elements</term> must occur, it is often necessary to apply a strong fire. Before one brings over the <term>Element of Fire</term> <note>original: "Elementum ignis"; the most subtle and transformative of the four elements</note>, everything must glow. Afterward, if one is to <term>calcine</term> <note>to reduce a substance to powder or ash by heat</note> the <term>dregs</term>, one must burn them in the <term>reverberatory furnace</term> <note>original: "reverber"; a furnace where the flame is reflected or "reverberated" onto the material, providing intense heat</note>. Thus, it often happens that before the <term>Matter</term> <note>original: "Materia"</note> becomes white, the <term>Matter</term> has turned to glass and is thus ruined, one thing with the other. 

But in the Great Work, this uncertainty does not exist; the <term>dregs</term> must consume themselves, as <term>Geber</term> <note>Latinized name of Jabir ibn Hayyan, a famous 8th-century polymath and "father of chemistry"</note> says: "The dragon shall eat its own blackness," and it shall be fed with its own <term>venom</term> <note>original: "Veneno"; referring to the toxic or corrosive potential of the raw matter that is transformed into medicine during the process</note>. Dandin says: "The black raven shall brood its own eggs—"

# On the Philosopher's Stone

...thus the hard bones, which were full of fat and marrow, are consumed in such a way that they become like a white, subtle dust, which is intangible between the fingers; Nature works this, as we see daily before our eyes. Where, then, does the stinking <term>matter</term> <note>original: "Materia"; the physical substance of the body undergoing decay</note> remain? It passes away and becomes nothing, and the element of Earth is thus cleansed and becomes white as snow, so that it is entirely intangible. Thus it is manifest that in our Art one need not separate any <term>elements</term> <note>original: "Elementa"</note>, nor require any washing or purification of the <term>mercury</term> <note>represented by the symbol ☿</note> except to test whether it is good and penetrating. I have presented this to you for this reason: so that you might understand that the <term>separation of the elements</term> <note>original: "separationes Elementorum"</note> is not necessary in our Work, nor is the <term>rectification</term> <note>original: "rectificatio"; the process of refining a substance through repeated distillation or chemical treatment</note>, because the <term>dregs</term> <note>original: "feces"; the impure sediment or remains</note> consume themselves, as has been heard. But in the <term>separation of the elements</term> <note>original: "separatione Elementorum"</note>, something is always lost in the fire, for they flee from the heat. And as easily as something is lost, it brings a disadvantage to the Work—which is not to be feared here, for in the <term>Great Work</term> <note>the "Magnum Opus," or the full process of creating the Philosopher's Stone</note> one must not separate any element.

Now further, you shall also know that one can make <term>oils</term> <note>original: "Oehle"; in alchemy, an "oil" refers to the concentrated essence or "soul" of a metal</note> from all metals, even without the separation of the elements and without much washing and <term>dissolving</term> <note>original: "solvierung"</note>. Yet it must be done with <term>strong waters</term> <note>original: "aquis fortibus"; typically refers to nitric acid or other corrosive mineral acids used to dissolve metals</note>, and one must give it a <term>ferment</term> <note>original: "ferment"; a substance added to trigger a specific chemical transformation or to "ripen" the mixture</note> if one wishes to make them from a perfect metal. However, I advise you that you should make no oil from any imperfect metal.

<summary>This page argues that nature naturally purifies substances into a white, subtle dust, meaning the alchemist does not need to manually separate elements or wash mercury, which risks losing material in the fire. It also introduces the possibility of creating medicinal or alchemical oils from perfect metals using "strong waters" and fermentation.</summary>

<keywords>natural purification, white dust, mercury, separation of elements, Great Work, alchemical oils, strong waters, fermentation, Isaac Hollandus</keywords>

until it passes the test.

the <term>imperfect metals</term> into <term>silver</term> <note>symbol: ☽</note>; and its
<term>projection</term> <note>the ratio of elixir to base metal</note> is 1 part to 1000 parts; for it is a
very powerful oil; I have seen wonders from it,
and I consider this oil to be a minor <term>medicine</term>.

->On the Philosophers' Stone <note>original Latin: "De lapide Philosophico"</note><-

## A Recipe

Found in a small chest that had been walled into a masonry wall, sold for a great deal of money; and it is true and very <term>expert</term> <note>original: "expert"; meaning proven or highly effective</note>, which those who are <term>skilled in alchemy</term> <note>original Latin: "periti alchimiæ"</note> will well recognize, as one shall indeed hear in the <term>process</term> <note>the step-by-step instructions</note>; therefore take care not to reveal such a <term>secret</term> to the greedy and the proud, nor to those who are not "children of the <term>Philosophers</term>" <note>a traditional term for sincere students of alchemy</note>. Therefore, begin thus:

Take in the name of our Lord Jesus Christ <term>Gold</term> <note>symbol: ☉</note> as much as you want; make an <term>amalgam</term> <note>a soft alloy produced by mixing silver or gold with mercury</note> with 1 part <term>Mercury</term> <note>symbol: ☿</note>, and add the part of gold. Let the mercury evaporate <note>original: "abrauchen"</note> with a small fire, afterwards <term>reverberate</term> it <note>original: "referberiers"; to heat a substance in a furnace so that the flames are deflected down onto it</note> for 20 days and nights; grind it very fine; pour onto it good distilled, rectified vinegar to the height of four fingers over the <term>matter</term>; seal the <term>cucurbit</term> <note>original: "cucurbitam"; a gourd-shaped glass vessel used for distillation</note> well; set it upon warm ashes for 2 days and nights; after that, the vinegar will gain a red color from the gold; then reverberate it again for 9 days as before—always reverberating for less time—and each time you should pour the colored vinegar together; do this as long and as often until all your gold comes into <term>resolution</term> <note>dissolves into a liquid state</note>, except for a grey powder which is worth nothing. This I have also done, but I used <term>strong water</term> <note>original Latin: "aquam fortem"; usually nitric acid, a powerful solvent</note> so that I might bring it into <term>solution</term> more easily.

Distill the <term>solution</term> <note>original Latin: "Solutum"</note>, and you will have your salt

### Of Isaac Hollandus

...found **Gold** <note>symbol: ☉</note> at the bottom / reverberate it for 12
hours (as the recipe commands) which I have
done; but I regret it / for then there went
the **Spirit** <note>the volatile essence or "soul" of the metal</note> away, more than half; I would have
received and captured the Spirit well, had
I known it.

Thereafter dissolve that **Gold** <note>symbol: ☉</note> in rectified *aqua vitæ* <note>Latin: "water of life"; highly distilled alcohol used as a solvent</note>, let it stand / as was said of the vinegar / what
remains undissolved / reverberate that for 3 days and
3 nights / and when all your **Gold** <note>symbol: ☉</note> is dissolved / in the
*aqua vitæ* / then pour the solutions together / 
distill the *aqua vitæ* from it / then you will find
a beautiful salt at the bottom / which immediately 
dissolves, just like salt of 12 <note>original: "Salz $Xij$"; likely referring to a specific weight or a salt of mercury</note> / of which salt
you shall take 1 part and 4 parts **Mercury** <note>symbol: ☿</note>; place
upon it a glowing coal / and also beneath it / 
then you will find **Mercury** <note>symbol: ☿</note> in half an hour / <term>calcined</term> <note>reduced to a dry powder through heat</note>
into a powder / but that would not "stand" <note>original: "gestehen"; meaning to solidify or coagulate properly</note> for me / 
and what hindered me / (as it seems to me /) was the
salt, because the <term>Spiritus</term> <note>Latin: Spirit</note>, as I have said / has
escaped / during the <term>reverberation</term> in the 12 hours 
mentioned before.

Now take the powder of the **Mercury** <note>abbreviated: "Merc:"</note> and **reverberate**
it for 3 days / then grind it and dissolve it in vinegar / 
reverberate it for a second time / and dissolve it in 
*aqua vitæ*, and then further 1 part to 4 parts
as before / and so on / as you wish to <term>augment</term> <note>to increase the quantity or potency of the alchemical substance</note> / 
then you shall take 1 part of the powder / 
thrown upon 4 parts of melted **Mercury** <note>abbreviated: "Merc:"</note> / it is good
**Gold** <note>symbol: ☉</note>; with **Silver** <note>symbol: ☾</note> you may also do this. Amen.

->## Of the Philosopher's Stone.<-

## This piece cost 800 Gulden. <note>original: "Gfl."; Gulden or Florins. This was an enormous sum of money in the 16th/17th century, emphasizing the perceived value of this secret.</note>

O Child of Teaching / first seek the Kingdom of GOD / in the obedience of the Most High in the Word of GOD / and dispose all your mind toward the poor / thus use this Philosophical Work / according to the canons <note>rules or established principles</note> or rules of **chemistry** <note>original: "chimiæ"</note> / a flux <note>a state of flowing or transformation</note> under Nature / and rejoice in God the Lord.

## Another Recipe.

**First**, draw off its moisture / having also been washed very well and clean from the start / <term>sublimate</term> <note>the process of heating a substance until it turns to vapor and then collects as a solid again, used for purification</note> it **by itself** <note>original: "per se"</note> without adding anything / as long as something rises / whatever remains lying there, cast it away. Sublimate it afterward with as much common salt / so that it may be clean and **prepared** / 6 or 7 times / thereafter lay it upon a glass plate / in a moist cellar / so that it dissolves / pour the dissolved liquid back in / until everything is dissolved / or shut it in a glass / and place it in the **cavity** <note>original: "sinum"; likely a warm sand bath or a specific part of the furnace</note>, whatever will not dissolve / sublimate that again with prepared salt / then you have the **Water of the Mercury of the Philosophers** <note>original: "aquam Merc: Philosophorum"</note>, of this take as much as you wish / and as much of the red, clean, pure **laton** <note>an alchemical term for a base metal or "dead" matter waiting to be "resurrected" into gold</note> / as it can consume / thereafter put it into a closed tower <note>a "philosopher's tower" or Athanor, a type of furnace</note> / and keep it for many days / you shall see in a short time / the **Crow's Head** <note>original: "Rabenhaupt"; the "Nigredo" or black stage of alchemy, representing the death/putrefaction of the matter</note> / which shall soon have white blossoms / thereafter yellow and at the end red.

# Isaac Hollandus

If you wish to follow God, I will teach you how to make the **Stone** from two **Lights** <note>In alchemy, the "Two Lights" usually refer to the Sun (Gold) and the Moon (Silver).</note> (as the Ancients and their followers made with their own hands) without hidden words <note>original: "bedeckte Wort"; meaning without the usual confusing allegories or "deck-namen" used to hide secrets</note>, [describing] the manual operation and its powers, and its extraction from its nature and first being or substance. For one cannot set it apart from its own operation which Nature has performed, or God in His final work; and it is made solely in these two Lights.

Although even if these two Lights had remained in the **mine** <note>OCR: "nimiris"; likely a corruption of the Latin *minera*, meaning the ore or the earth where minerals are found</note> until Judgment Day where they first emerged, Nature would not have worked [this transformation] within them, for Nature has already applied its utmost power therein. Therefore, we wish to bring these two Lights directly out of Nature into a better one, out of a base nature and form. For this reason, one must go backward and behind oneself, and **deconstruct** <note>original: "entmachen"; literally to "un-make" or reverse the natural process of solidification</note> that which was joined together, which Nature had worked, and bring it back again into the form it was in when Nature first began to work; and into such a form it must be brought. We follow Nature with **Art** <note>meaning the "Great Art" of Alchemy</note> as far as we are able, and we must sometimes overstep Nature... go to the **trees** <note>Likely referring to the "Philosophical Tree," the crystalline structures that grow during alchemical crystallization</note>... divide, **rectify** <term>to purify a substance, usually a liquid, by repeated distillation to reach its most potent state</term>, **dissolve**... <note>original: "solvi-"; catchword for "solviren"</note>

<vocab>Stone, Lights, Nature, substance, Form, Art, rectify, dissolve</vocab>

<summary>Isaac Hollandus explains that the Philosopher's Stone is created from the "Two Lights" (Gold and Silver) by using Alchemical Art to "un-make" their finished natural forms, returning them to their primal state for further perfection.</summary>
<keywords>Isaac Hollandus, Two Lights, Alchemical Art vs Nature, Deconstruction, Rectification, Philosophical Tree, Prime Matter</keywords>

### Concerning the Philosophers' Stone.

[to] refine, congeal, clean the earth and join it together, wax <note>original: "inceriren"; an alchemical process of softening a substance to a waxy consistency</note> and many other joinings and many other works which Nature cannot perform; for she is not moved to such works; therefore one must transfer the <term>Matter</term> <note>original: "Materia"</note> into a better form than Nature has provided.

Afterward, one must know how you should strip from the <term>Moon</term> <note>the alchemical symbol ☾, representing Silver</note> its combustible sulfur; for the <term>Moon</term> is an imperfect body—cold, moist, and coarse—containing much moisture with blackness; and it is very earthy, coarse, and feminine because of its coldness; for this reason, the <term>Moon</term> always thirsts <note>original: "dürmet," likely "thirsts"</note> for the <term>Sun</term> <note>the alchemical symbol ☉, representing Gold</note>. Therefore, one must first strip and cast away from the <term>Moon</term> its combustible <term>Antimony</term> <note>the symbol ♁; here referring to the "earthy" impurities or the mineral dross</note>, earthiness, and blackness.

Thus, take as much of the <term>Moon</term> [Silver] as you wish; refine it on the cupel <note>original: "auff dem Test"; a porous vessel used to refine precious metals</note> by blowing off impurities, beat it into thin plates, then take sublimated <term>Orpiment</term> <note>original: "Auripigmentum"; a yellow arsenic sulfide mineral</note>. The middle <term>Matter</term> prepared to the highest degree, as has been taught in the work regarding it; grind it small upon a stone with distilled vinegar until it is like a paste; coat the <term>silver plates</term> <note>original: "lamines lunæ"</note>, dry them in the sun, by the fire, or in the room;

put them into a crucible, one upon the other, until the crucible is full; seal it with luting clay <note>original: "lutiers"; an airtight seal used in alchemy</note>, put it in a calcining furnace with a small, gentle fire so that the <term>Moon</term> [Silver] does not melt for 12 hours; let it grow cold; crush it in an iron mortar or scrape off the calcined material; and if it is not yet sufficiently calcined, then calcine it once more, or as long as it takes until the <term>Moon</term> is calcined; grind it on a stone with common sublimated water; then wash...

# Isaac Hollandus

In a glass bowl, wash its blackness away with common distilled water <note>original: "Wasser communi distillata"</note> until the water flows clear again from the powder. Then dry it by the sun or fire and keep it in a glass vessel <note>original: "in vitro"</note>. Let the water with which the <term>Moon</term> <note>the alchemical symbol ☽, representing Silver</note> was washed evaporate; drive the residue <note>original: "feces"</note> onto the remainder; in this way you lose nothing. Then you may file your <term>Moon</term> [Silver] small and <term>take</term> <note>the symbol ℞, used for recipes</note> for 1 mark <note>a unit of weight, approx. 230 grams</note> of Silver, 1 ounce of prepared arsenic; layer them <note>original: "stratificirs"; placing alternating layers of metal and chemical in a crucible</note> and calcine <note>heat to a high temperature without melting to cause oxidation or change</note> for 12 or 16 hours. Afterward, grind it and wash it as I have taught you before:

Afterward, take the powder of the <term>Moon</term> [Silver]; heat it to a red glow for 24 or 32 hours; then calcine it in the furnace in which one calcines spirits for 12 days, with such heat that it glows, but not entirely. When the 12 days are over, then let it glow gently—brown, red—but so that it does not melt, for 3 days. Let it cool; then it is ready.

For the <term>Moon</term> [Silver] has two sicknesses <note>original: "Siechthumb"; an archaic term for illness or infirmity</note> like other imperfect metals, but these two sicknesses do not reach into the innermost root as they do in other imperfect metals. One sickness is the combustible <term>Sulfur</term>; the other is coldness and moisture. The first sickness is removed through calcining with arsenic and washing. Know that the substance of arsenic is coarse, such that it burns all "bodies" <note>original: "Leichnamb"; referring to the solid metallic form</note> and brings them to nothing. <term>Orpiment</term> <note>arsenic trisulfide</note> and <term>Sulfur</term> do the same; these three are of one nature.

And when the arsenic and orpiment are lightened and cleansed of their impur- <margin>ity</margin>

...bility, thickness, and wildness of our spirit, and you take the middle spirit. Now the <term>matter</term> is ready to extract the <term>Stone</term> <note>original: "lapidem"</note>. # Of the Philosopher's Stone. 73

E v

...impurity, thickness, and wild, unclean spirits, taking from them the "middle spirit." With this spirit, you drive away the combustibility of the **sulfur** <term>sulfur: in alchemy, the active principle of fire and inflammability</term> of the metals, through **calcination** <term>calcining: heating a substance to high temperatures to reduce it to a powder</term>, **washing** <note>original: "abluiren"</note>, **levigation** <term>levigating: grinding a substance into a fine powder, often while moist</term>, **purification** <note>original: "purgiren"</note>, and **repetition** <note>original: "reiteriren"</note>. In this way, **arsenic** <term>arsenicum</term> and **orpiment** <term>auripigmentum: a yellow arsenic sulfide mineral</term> are compared to **Theriac** <term>Tyriacks: a famous ancient "universal antidote" containing many ingredients, including poisons like viper flesh, rendered safe through processing</term>. For when the **poison** <note>original Latin: "Venenum"</note> in the Theriac is not properly prepared, it kills the human; but when it is prepared, it drives the poison out. **Arsenic** and **orpiment** act in the same way.

Prepare the **Sun** <note>the symbol ☉, representing Gold</note> into a most subtle powder, as was taught in the work concerning the **Moon** <note>the symbol ☾, representing Silver</note>, in **this manner** <note>original Latin: "hoc modo"</note>: **Take** <note>the recipe symbol ℞</note> prepared **Gold** and **Silver** and mix them together, grinding them on a stone like a painter's pigment with water. Dry it again, then **reverberate** <note>heating the material in a furnace where the flames are reflected or "reverberated" back onto the substance</note> it in a broad refining dish until it swells like a sponge. Let it cool; take it out; **dissolve** <note>original: "solvirts"</note> it in vinegar and distill it in a **water bath** <term>Balneo: a gentle heating method using a vessel of hot water to ensure even temperature</term>, as was described in the work of the **Moon**. Then dissolve it again. Continue this **dissolving** and **congealing** <term>coaguliren: the process of turning a liquid back into a solid form</term> until no **dregs** <note>original Latin: "feces"</note> remain behind. Then the work is fulfilled. As **Dandin** <note>likely referring to an alchemical authority or a specific aphorism</note> says: "Unlock that which is locked and make it wet; and that which is unlocked, lock it and dry it." Do the same with the coldness of the **Moon**, and you shall find the warmth of the **Sun**, and you shall find the coldness of the Moon.

Now the matter is ready to extract the **Stone** <note>original Latin: "lapidem," the Philosopher's Stone</note>. The matter of Gold shall for the most part climb or ascend, and you shall take with it all the subtle parts of the **Moon**. Nothing shall remain behind in the climbing, or [little...] <meta>The catchword "nig/" suggests the next page begins with "wenig" (little/small amount).</meta>

    *   *Text:* "De lapide Philosophico."
        *   *Translation:* On the Philosophical Stone.
    *   *Text:* "haltet alle die feces , die in der sublimation dahinden bleiben/ denn ihr müst sie wieder reverberiren/denn es ist des Leichnams lun. welcher noch nicht aufklummen ist."
        *   *Vocab check:* feces = dregs/residue; sublimation = process of turning solid to gas back to solid; reverberiren = heat with reflected flame (reverberatory furnace); Leichnamb = corpse (the fixed matter); lun. (Luna) = Silver or the lunar aspect.

# On the Philosophical Stone.

Keep all the <term>feces</term> <note>original: "feces," referring to the solid dregs or dross remaining after a chemical process</note> which remain behind in the <term>sublimation</term> <note>the process of heating a substance into a vapor so it collects again as a purified solid</note>; for you must <term>reverberate</term> <note>to heat a substance in a furnace where the flame is deflected downward onto the material</note> them again, because it is the <term>Luna</term> <note>the alchemical name for Silver, representing the white, purified stage of the work</note> of the body <note>original: "Leichnams," literally "corpse," referring to the fixed, solid matter being worked upon</note> which has not yet risen up. You shall <term>solve</term> <note>dissolve into liquid form</note> the body and the <term>ferment</term> <note>a substance used to trigger the transformation of the base matter into the Stone</note> of our Stone in this way: [extract] the body out of the residues with distilled vinegar, as much as will dissolve; then coagulate it, then reverberate it for 24 hours with a gentle fire, so that it glows no more than red-hot; let it cool.

Then take it out and dissolve it again with distilled vinegar; coagulate it, and reverberate it for 5 or 6 hours, so that it glows no more than red-hot; let it cool. Dissolve it again in common water; coagulate it again; afterward, let it glow a faint red for the duration of 5 or 6 <term>Pater Nosters</term> <note>a common medieval and early modern unit of time: the length of time it takes to recite the "Our Father" prayer</note>; then take it out again and dissolve it in common water. Dissolve it as often as necessary until nothing more remains behind; if it is not so, dissolve and coagulate it again until nearly all the moisture is evaporated and distilled; then let it cool. When it is cold, it should come together in pieces as long as a finger, clear as crystal and white as snow.

This manual operation must be done in glass dishes, closed up, standing upon a boiling <term>balneo</term> <note>original: "Balneo," a water bath or bain-marie, providing gentle, even heat</note>; and you shall cover the vessel with a double linen cloth, so that no dust or impurity falls into it. Our work must be clean to the very highest degree, once you have prepared your <term>preciosam ferram</term> <note>original Latin: "precious tool" or "precious iron"; though likely a scribal error for "preciosam terram," meaning precious earth</note> and body, and your ferment to receive the soul

<meta>The catchword "und" (and) indicates the text continues on the next page, likely describing the union of the soul with the body.</meta>

<summary>This page describes the meticulous process of purifying the "corpse" or solid residue of the work through repeated cycles of dissolution in vinegar and water, followed by "reverberating" heat. The goal is to produce a crystalline, snow-white substance that serves as the base for the Philosophical Stone.</summary>

# Concerning the Philosopher’s Stone <note>original Latin: *De lapide Philosophico*</note>

From pure water, nothing comes but pure water. It may well be purified by chance <note>original: "bey Ebenthuer," likely meaning through trial or experimentation</note> if it contains <term>feces</term> <note>original: "feces," meaning the dregs, dross, or impurities left at the bottom of a vessel</note>, which you might extract and cleanse with your arts; then that small amount of water is better than it was at first, but it remains water, and you can make nothing else out of it.

So it is with the <term>Sun and Moon</term> <note>original: "sol. und lun.", referring to the alchemical names for gold and silver</note> if you wish to work with each one alone. The Sun is Sun and remains Sun; the Moon is Moon and remains so—unless you make them spiritual and add to them their own <term>corpse</term> <note>the solid, physical body of the metal</note> to fix their own spirit, so that it might perform a <term>projection</term> <note>the final stage of alchemy where the "Stone" is cast upon base metals to transform them</note> upon cleansed metals in the third order. But it is not the Philosopher’s Stone; it is a simple medicine of the third order and nothing else.

And the Stone must be made from spiritual things that one may separate, and from two natures, cold and hot: from snow and light, from white and red, from coarse and <term>subtle</term> <note>original: "subtil," referring to substances that are refined, volatile, or ethereal</note>, from male and female. And then the one shall work into the nature of the other: the coarse made subtle, and the subtle coarse; the light made heavy, the warm made cold, and the moist made dry, and the dry made moist. Thus one nature works into the other until finally, at the end, all things are brought into one simple nature. And then every thing shall be in a better state, and in a better, higher form, or even in another <term>substance</term>, and then in the end all

<meta>catchword: Thing</meta>

<summary>This page explains that basic substances like water or pure gold and silver cannot become the Philosopher's Stone on their own; they must be combined with their opposites (hot/cold, male/female) to reach a higher state of existence.</summary>
<keywords>purification, gold and silver, Sol and Luna, projection, third order medicine, union of opposites, subtle and coarse, transformation</keywords>

# Isaac Hollandus <note>original Latin: *Isaaci Hollandi*</note>

...brought into a simple and single <term>substance</term> <note>original: "substantz," referring to the essential, underlying reality of the matter</note>, then the thing is perfect, just as the unconquerable heaven is simple. But the Earth requires moisture, which the heaven provides in abundance; so it is also with heat and cold.

Everything that the Earth lacks, the heaven can give in abundance; yet the heaven is neither cold nor warm, neither moist nor dry—it is a <term>simple body</term> <note>original: "simpel Leichnamb," meaning a pure, uncompounded physical form or "corpse"</note> and perfect in the highest perfection, wherein it shall always remain. Within it are contained all perfection and properties. Thus, everything that the earth, herbs, trees, humans, and animals have need of, the heaven gives in abundance. So it is also with heat and cold; and everything the Earth lacks, the heaven can give in abundance through its great power, which God has placed within it through His divine ordination and wonderful omnipotence.

Thus, you shall learn to recognize the **Stone** as a simple medicine of the third order <note>The "third order" represents the final, most potent stage of alchemical medicine, capable of instant transformation</note>. For our Stone must possess the property which God has invisibly instilled into the heaven. Thus, we must pour that same property into our **Philosophical Mastery** <note>original: "Philosophischen Meisterschafft," referring to the specific alchemical technique or the "Great Work" itself</note>, so that it may have the same property and mastery found in a simple body. And when it possesses all this property and power, only then is it truly our **Stone**.

<summary>This page describes the Philosopher's Stone as a "simple" substance that mirrors the heavens, providing all necessary qualities (heat, cold, moisture, dryness) without being limited by them. It emphasizes that the Stone must reach a state of divine perfection and "simplicity" to function as a medicine of the third order.</summary>

-># On the Philosopher’s Stone <note>original Latin: *De lapide Philosophico*</note><-

<p align="center">F</p>

<meta>catchword: Thus</meta>

Thus I have taught you how and from what the Stone should be made, and what quality it must possess before it may take the name of the Stone.

Now take the <term>corpse</term> <note>original: *Leichnamb*, referring to the solid base or "dead" matter remaining after distillation</note>, or earth, or **ferment**, and weigh it, and write it in a book, and put it into such a glass. Then take 8 parts of **ferment**—that is, if you have 8 ounces <note>original symbol: ℥</note> of **ferment**, weigh 1 dram <note>original symbol: ʒ i</note> of your **sublimation**—and put it into a small glass. Pour distilled water upon the **ferment**; place a ground glass cap upon each, fitted to the mouth of the glass. Place each in a warm **bath** <note>original: *balneum*, a water bath used for gentle, even heating</note>, and it will immediately **dissolve** <note>original: *solviren*</note> into a clear water. When they are **dissolved**, pour the two waters together into one glass and stir them well together; they will mix easily, for they desire friendship with one another. The husband desires his own wife, and the wife her own husband; the dry, cold earth desires the warm moisture of the water. Thus one desires to embrace the other, and the one to enter the other, down to the ground into its innermost root. And thus there is then a **perfect mixture** <note>original: *perfectè Mengung*</note>; yet, it is still not yet a **perfect mixture**, for with water and water there is no hindrance <note>original: *Hinternuß*</note>. It joins together at once without any delay, because each desires to receive the other because of their great purity, which they both possess, and one loves the other.

# Isaac Hollandus <note>original Latin: *Isaaci Hollandi*</note>

# Concerning the Philosopher’s Stone <note>original Latin: *De lapide Philosophico*</note>
->F ij<-
<meta>catchword: meal</meta>

**Salt** must have a part with the **spirit**, for the **Salt** has a soul within it; therefore the ancients called the Salt "ensouled," because it holds a soul within it. Thus it is a **mediator** <note>original: *Mittel*; a substance that allows two opposing natures, like fire and water, to join</note> between the **corpse** <note>original: *Leichnamb*; the solid, physical matter or residue remaining in the vessel</note>, **soul**, and **spirit**. It must share a part with them; it must, with its sharpness, keep the spirit, soul, and corpse together so that one may enter into the other. If the salt is not drawn out of the corpse along with the spirit, and the salt does not lead or guide it—carrying a soul within it—then it has no part with the spirit, and it remains lying hidden in the deepest part of the corpse. 

Therefore, one must draw the **salt** out of the corpse with the spirit, having within it a soul and spirit; herewith it must **ascend** <note>original: *aufffahren*; to vaporize or rise during heating in a still</note> and become one single thing with them. It shall then be a mediator between the spirit and the corpse, for the salt has both soul and corpse within it. It cannot live without the soul belonging to it, and the soul cannot exist without the spirit; thus the salt must be one thing with the spirit, and the spirit one with the soul. Therefore, the salt is a mediator between these, so long as they wish to remain together.

*Arnaldus de Villa Nova* <note>a famous 13th-century physician and alchemist</note> says that the salt is marvelous, and through these aforementioned words it is manifest that no **fixation** <note>the alchemical process of making a volatile substance stable and resistant to fire</note> may occur—remaining **perfect** <note>original: *perfect*</note> in the end—if the salt is not drawn out of the corpse with the spirit, as has been described. They fail entirely—

# Multiplication. <note>original: "Multiplicatio." In alchemy, this is the process of increasing the quantity or the transmutative power of the Philosopher's Stone.</note>

To multiply your **Stone** in its **power**, but not in its **size**—that is, so that it shall be exalted in its **nobility** and **subtlety** <note>refining a substance into its most pure, fine, and penetrating form</note>—so that it now effects 10,000 in the **projection** <note>the final act of casting the Stone upon base metals to transmute them</note>; when it is multiplied, refined, and exalted in its size and power, then its projection effects 1,000,000.

And when you multiply the **Stone** three times, and make it subtle in its power, then its projection shall be without end, because of its great subtlety: So much so that the **Stone** might be multiplied so often that one would never again reach the end of its power of projection.

But one might also multiply it so many times that one could not keep it in any glass; it would pass through it, and if you wished to **dissolve** <note>reduce to liquid form</note> it, it would pass invisibly through the glass, and the glass would remain whole, yet the glass would be changed in color into a **ruby**. For set the glass to melt, as the **Stone** is made, and cast the **Stone** upon it—as much powder as you ought to take—as if you wished to change <term>Jupiter <note>the alchemical symbol ♃, representing the metal tin</note></term> into <term>Sol <note>Latin for the Sun, the alchemical symbol for gold</note></term>. For what is lacking in the Earth, Heaven can provide, yet it is not of its own [earthly] nature that it gives to us; so it is also in our **Stone**: it makes all dry bodies moist, and all moist ones dry; all cold things warm, and all warm things cold; all unclean things clean, and unspeakably much more.

# On the Philosopher's Stone <note>original: "De lapide Philosophico"</note>

...once you have distilled everything with the strong water <note>Aqua Fortis</note>, then you have a precious red water, glowing at night like fire and lightning. Then rectify your water in a **water bath** <term>Balneo</term> <note>a "bain-marie"; a vessel of hot water used to heat laboratory glassware gently and evenly</note> several times by pouring it off and pouring it on, until at last you have distilled everything out of the water bath. In this way, a precious water is prepared, in which you shall multiply and open your **Stone** <term>Stein</term>. Take your blessed Stone, dissolve <note>original: "solvirt"</note> it in this water, and it will dissolve immediately. Place it in a lukewarm water bath, with the **alembic** <term>alambic</term> <note>the "head" or cap of a still used for distilling</note> and **receiver** <term>receptacul</term> <note>the vessel intended to catch the distilled liquid</note> firmly **sealed** <term>verlutirt</term> <note>hermetically sealed using "lute," a specialized clay or paste for airtight joints</note> all around, and keep it warm.

When your **Aqua Fortis** <term>AF</term> has distilled over, test the powder from the glass pot. Take a little bit out, place it on a glowing **metal plate** <term>Lamel</term>, and observe whether it "flies" <note>vaporizes or sublimes</note>, as I have taught elsewhere. Pour the water back onto it, seal it again, and distill as before, until it no longer distills. Afterward, test and prove it again as before, to see if it now flies away a little.

If it does not fly away more than at first, pour the **strong water** <term>Aq. f.</term> onto it again, place it in the water bath, and do as was previously taught. Repeat this **manual operation** <term>Handwürckung</term> so often until everything flies off from the glowing plates; then it is sufficiently opened and clarified <note>literally "blown open" or "un-glassed"</note>. Then **sublimate** <term>sublimirt</term> <note>to heat a solid until it turns into vapor and then solidifies again, a method of high-level purification</note> it in every way as before, 3 or 4 times, each time without adding anything, for no **dregs** <term>feces</term> <note>sediment or impurities</note> are necessary; then it is enough.

# Isaac Hollandus <note>Isaac Hollandus was a legendary 15th-century alchemist, though these texts likely date from later; his works emphasize the practical, laboratory-based "Great Work."</note>

For take a small part of that which has been **sublimated** <term>sublimirt</term> <note>purified by heating a substance until it vaporizes and then allowing it to solidify again</note> and set it to be **fixed** <term>figiren</term> <note>the process of making a volatile substance stable under heat so it no longer vaporizes</note>. Give it a small fire or warmth at first, and always increase the fire by degrees, just as is taught in the Great Work. 

When it is **fixed** <term>fix</term>, weigh it to see how much has been fixed, and then add one part of the Spirit to three parts of the fixed material. Set it to be fixed again as was taught before. Do this until everything that was sublimated is fixed. It will be fixed immediately once it is entirely fixed in all its parts; and the other parts that are added to the first part will become fixed more easily than the first, for the one part fixes the other part.

And in this **multiplication** <term>multiplication</term> <note>the process of increasing the quantity and power of the Philosopher’s Stone</note>, no blackness appears, nor do any colors of the world reveal themselves, nor even whiteness. For it is nothing but a sublimation, just like the simple heaven, invincible, for it is a simple matter. 

But although it is sublimated, it is nevertheless fixed, for it is as invincible as the heaven—a simple, clarified **Body** <term>Leichnamb</term> <note>original: "Leichnamb"; refers to the solid, physical remains or "salt" of the substance</note>. For that which is to perform the **projection** <term>projection</term> <note>the final step where a tiny amount of the Stone is thrown onto base metal to transmute it into gold</note> must be Spirit, Soul, and Body, all equally strong. <meta>This page continues the technical and philosophical explanation of the Philosopher’s Stone. While the previous page focused on the physical "multiplication" and "fixing" of the substance in a furnace, this page moves into the theory of the Stone’s tripartite nature—Body, Soul, and Spirit—comparing its unity to the Holy Trinity.</meta>

# -> On the Philosopher’s Stone <- <note>original Latin: "de lapide Philosophico"</note>

Body <term>Leichnamb</term> <note>In alchemy, the "body" refers to the solid, physical matter or the metallic base of the Stone, as opposed to its volatile "spirits."</note>, so it would be a contrary nature, in that it would not be a single thing; and yet it is not removed from its nature into another nature, for thus it must be. And if it were not so, then it would perform no **projection** <term>projection</term> <note>the final act of alchemy: casting the finished Stone upon base metal to transform it into gold or silver</note>. The Stone must be **sublimated** <term>sublimiren</term>; thus it is placed from a fixed nature into a spiritual nature. Our Body is a Body, Soul, and Spirit; and these three are one thing, indivisible from one another, in a great power, and one substance and one nature. They are in one just like the **Holy Trinity**—Father, Son, and Holy Spirit—three in one, with the power and unity of the simple substance, where one is not without the other. The Father is not the Son, likewise the Son is not the Holy Spirit, nor is the Holy Spirit the Father; yet nevertheless, three Persons are one simple, eternal substance.

And thus our Stone is neither fixed nor volatile; it is both Spirit and Body. It must have both in its nature, equally strong, as it is taught. And it seems to be fixed so as to endure the fire; that comes through its firmness into a state of compression, so that the parts of the Stone are thus pressed densely into one Being and are entirely compact. And in addition, there is no subtler and cleaner thing in the world; and the cleaner and subtler a thing is, so much more are the subtle and clean things firmly set and penetrated, and the heavier they are in weight.

<meta>catchword: For</meta>

<summary>The author explains the tripartite nature of the Philosopher's Stone (Body, Soul, and Spirit) by comparing it to the Holy Trinity, emphasizing that it must be both spiritual and physical to be effective in transmutation.</summary>
<keywords>Philosopher's Stone, Holy Trinity, Body Soul and Spirit, sublimation, projection, fixed and volatile, alchemy theory, Isaac Hollandus</keywords>

<meta>This page continues the technical and philosophical explanation of the Philosopher’s Stone. Having previously established the Stone as a tripartite unity of Body, Soul, and Spirit comparable to the Trinity, the author now explains how this unity grants the Stone physical properties—specifically its resistance to fire and its extreme "subtlety" or volatility.</meta>

### Isaaci Hollandi

For they love each other, so that they are confirmed into one, and are difficult to separate from each other. See to it that its goodness <note>its intrinsic quality or "virtue"</note> withstands the fire, and the fire cannot overcome its parts, for they are so firm; yet nonetheless it is a pure spirit, for it flies <note>in alchemy, "to fly" refers to evaporation or sublimation—the transition from solid to gas</note> much more easily, once it is opened, than any other of all spirits; this is because of its great **subtlety** <term>Subtiligkeit</term> <note>the degree to which a substance is refined, ethereal, or "fine" rather than coarse</note> and the purity which it possesses in its nature.

How then should our Stone not fight better and more powerfully against the fire? For it is both; it is made of the **Body** <term>Leichnamb</term> <note>the material or physical substrate</note> and of the **Spirit**; since they are both one, the Stone shall withstand the fire so much more powerfully and mightily than gold, which remains as nature has crafted it; and it [gold] is a pure spirit and also resists the fire; therefore our Stone must withstand the fire a thousand times more strongly than Gold <term>☉</term> <note>the astrological and alchemical symbol for the Sun and Gold</note>. When our Stone is "opened" and unlocked with our **burning water** <term>brennenden Wasser</term> <note>a potent chemical solvent, such as an acid or distilled alcohol, used to dissolve the Stone's components</note>, which separates its compressed parts from one another, it swells up like a sponge; then the Gold <term>☉</term> flies up with a small fire, more easily than any spirit, because of the great subtlety that God has given it. However, our Stone shall fly upwards even more easily, with less heat and a smaller fire than gold <note>original Latin: "sol"</note>, for our Stone yields the very highest subtlety which God has given it,

Thus the ancients were accustomed to making the Stone as was taught before, before the art of **strong water** <term>▽</term> <note>original: "starcken ▽"; a symbol for Aqua Fortis or nitric acid, a powerful solvent</note> had been discovered. They worked thereafter as was taught before, and when the Stone was perfected, they did not yet have the true art to make and multiply the Stone in its own power alone. Rather, they worked it only as much as was needed after its **projection**, to make it **subtle** through **dissolution** in strong **distilled vinegar**, then through **coagulation**, until it stood as a beautiful **oil** <term>Oehl</term>. 

And when they desired to make the oil hard again, into a **mass** <term>Massam</term>, they took gold, one part **disembodied** <note>original: "entleichnambt"; a term for stripping a metal of its physical form to reach its essence</note> and made red as blood; and they mixed their dissolved oil together and **coagulated** it, and placed it in a secret furnace with tempered heat or warmth, until it was changed again into a dark **mass**, red as **dragon’s blood** <note>a traditional name for a deep red color or a specific resin, here describing the perfected red of the Stone</note>. ### Concerning the Philosophical Stone
<note>original Latin: "De lapide Philosophico"</note>

...put it in a crucible to test it: upon 1 pound of **Mercury** <note>original: ☿</note> and fine **Silver** <note>original: ☽</note> together / and cast your **white Stone** upon it / thus shall such **Mercury** be transmuted into true **Silver** / more or less according to the subtlety of the Stone / as you will hear hereafter / if you wish to cast your **red Stone** / then cast it upon **Silver** / for it is not necessary to add anything else / only to melt it / and cast your **red Stone** upon it / then the **Silver** transforms into true **Gold** <note>original: ☉</note> / better than that which may come out of the mines.

Always cast your **white Stone** upon **Mercury** and not upon other metals / for **Mercury** is nearest to **Silver** / but it is rawer and an uncooked **body** <term>Leichnamb</term> <note>literally "corpse"; refers to the physical, "dead" metallic matter before it is "vivified" by the Stone</note> / and the Stone needs to do nothing else / but to fully cook the **Mercury** / as I have taught in other places. Therefore, one should cast the Stone upon **Mercury** / because **Mercury** is closest to **Silver**.

It emphasizes that the transformation must follow a natural order—moving from imperfect metals to Silver, and only then to Gold—mirroring the previous page's discussion on the "white" and "red" stages of the Stone.</meta>

-># Isaac Hollandus<-

...nothing but **Gold** <note>original symbol: ☉</note>. One cannot go from the first degree to the third; one must first pass through the second. Thus the imperfect metals must first be brought to the state of **Silver** <note>original symbol: ☽</note>, and afterward to **Gold**. For no metal can reach the state of **Gold** unless it first becomes **Silver**, because **Gold** was always **Silver** before it became **Gold**. Nature must first create **Silver** before it is brought to the state of the **Sun** <note>Gold; original: "sol"</note>, for one cannot travel a hundred miles unless one has first walked the road that lies between.

But cast the **White Stone** upon whatever metal you wish, and it becomes **Silver**—provided that you make your metal "alive" and instill your **Soul** into it, as <term>Morienus</term> <note>A legendary 7th-century hermit and alchemist, often cited as the teacher of Prince Khalid ibn Yazid</note> says: "Our metal is not a common metal, for it lives and has a soul."

For one cannot go from the first degree to the third, as is taught in the **Vegetable, Animal, and Mineral** <note>original Latin: "Vegetabili animali und minerali"; the three kingdoms of nature through which alchemical principles are applied</note> realms. Afterward, one must know the manner of the <term>projection</term> <note>the final act of casting the medicinal Stone onto base metals to effect transmutation</note>. If you cast the Stone upon **Silver** or upon **Jupiter** <note>Tin; original symbol: ♃</note>, you will achieve nothing—even if you have already instilled a soul into the metals, or even if it were a living **body** <term>Leichnamb</term> <note>literally "corpse"; here referring to the physical matter of the metal that awaits vivification</note>, as **Silver** is. The Stone must be mixed in such a way that it penetrates subtly and firmly, even if it was a powder. And when the metal is melted thick and beautifully, the Stone nonetheless remains atop the metals when it is cast upon them (and when it then remained upon the metals, as it was cast...

no part with the **metals** / although it [once] was **metal**...

### Isaac Hollandus
<note>original: Isaaci Hollandi</note>

...for these two unclean, stinking metals <note>likely referring to lead and tin, or perhaps sulfur and mercury in their base states</note> have in them this unclean, living **Body** <note>original: "Leichnamb," literally "corpse"; in alchemy, this refers to the material, physical shell of a substance</note>, which is one with them—as the soul and the body are one—and when the **Stone**, whether white or red, is cast upon them, they also have within themselves that same living **Body**; afterward, they can be joined together. Thus, each thing draws that which belongs to its own nature, so that a joining occurs and one enters into the other. Then, the **Stone** expels all their corruption and creates a perfect living **Body**, better than those found in the minerals <note>meaning more perfect than naturally occurring gold or silver</note>.

Now I will teach you how you shall prepare the **Stone**, whether white or red, so that it may join with the coarse metals and enter into them. This happens thus: take 1 part of the red **Stone** and 1000 parts of **Gold** <term>☉</term> <note>original: ☉, the astrological and alchemical symbol for the Sun and Gold</note>. Beat the gold into thin plates. Afterward, take **sulfur** <term>sulph.</term> and grind it with a stone into a thick paste with **olive oil** <note>original: *oleo olivi*</note>. Smear your plates <note>original: *lamines*</note> with this, let them dry, and place them on warm coals to **calcine** them <term>calcinirt</term> <note>the process of heating a substance to high temperatures to reduce it to a friable powder</note>. Afterward, **pulverize** them <term>pulveriſirt</term> and wash that powder with common water until the water runs clear and clean away from the powder. Furthermore, grind the powder on a stone with honey or with **gum water** <note>water thickened with gum arabic</note>, just as a painter grinds his pigments. Afterward, put it again into a glass bowl and wash it once more with common **distilled water**. The powder will sink; pour off the water. Do this as many times as necessary until the water comes away clear from the powder. Afterward, dry it in the **Sun**—

### On the Philosopher’s Stone
<note>original: De lapide Philosophico</note>

...[continuing from previous page] or on a small fire, then your gold is ready.

Next, take 1000 parts of this powder and 1 part of the **Stone**, finely pulverized; mix them together. Then take a melting crucible that can well withstand a good, strong fire <term>△</term> <note>original symbol: △, representing the element of fire</note>. Upon this crucible, fit another melting crucible, like a closed bowl; grind them both on a stone so that they fit perfectly together. Place your powder inside; seal [lute] the crucible strongly and well, inside and out, to the thickness of 3 or 4 fingers. Dry the luting clay <term>lutum</term> <note>a specialized paste or cement used by alchemists to make vessels airtight</note> well. Afterward, place it in another crucible in a wind-furnace—first with a small fire <term>△</term>, then increasingly stronger, degree by degree, until the crucible glows thoroughly. Then increase your fire <term>△</term> significantly until you are certain that the matter <term>Materia</term> melts in the crucible. Keep the crucible at this same heat for 3 days, so that the matter remains constantly in a liquid state.

Afterward, let it cool by its own accord. Break open the crucible, and you will find a hard mass <note>original: "Massam"</note>, brittle as glass, which can then be pulverized. And if you then wish to perform projection <term>projection</term> <note>the final, climactic act of alchemy where the Stone is cast upon base metal to transform it</note>, take one part of this powder and 1000 parts of the clean metal—which has been endowed with a soul and made alive—and it transmutes <term>transmutirt</term> it into true Gold <term>☉</term> and Silver <term>☾</term> <note>original symbols: ☉ for the Sun/Gold and ☾ for the Moon/Silver</note>, better than that which is mined from the earth. And this is the "Work of Three Days" of which the Ancients have written in their books; it is the work that makes the coarse bodies <note>original: "Leichnamb," literally "corpse," used here to mean the physical shell of the metal</note> so subtle in only 3 days.

# On the Philosopher's Stone.

...innermost furnace <note>original: "nersten Ofen", likely the conclusion of "innersten Ofen" from the previous page</note> can barely endure; and keep it thus for 5 or 6 days. Thereafter, increase the fire so that the uppermost or outer furnace hisses when touched with a wet finger; maintain this for 3 days, then let it cool of its own accord. Take it out and break the glass; there you will find a hard **mass** <term>Massam</term> <note>original: "Massam", the Latin term for a solid lump of alchemical material</note>. If you wish to perform a **projection** <term>projection</term> <note>the act of casting the alchemical substance onto base metals to transform them</note> upon Mercury <term>☿</term>, put it in a crucible, then take this **mass**, pulverize it, and cast it upon the metal. Thereafter, increase the fire so that it begins to drive; let it drive for so long until it settles into a "King" <term>Könige</term> <note>a "regulus" or button of refined metal found at the bottom of the crucible after smelting</note>. See if it is malleable; if it is, you have accomplished the work and have good **Gold** <term>sol</term> <note>original: "sol", the Latin name and symbol ☉ for Gold</note>.

And note: as long as it is as brittle as glass, it is still **Medicine** <term>medicin</term> <note>the concentrated, potent form of the Stone before it has been fully tempered for metal transformation</note>. Thereafter, cast it upon melted Silver <term>☽</term> <note>original: "☽", the symbol for Silver</note> until the Silver becomes malleable and remains whole under the hammer; then you have good Gold <term>☉</term>. But as long as it is brittle, you have not yet reached the end of the **projection**, whether it be from the white or the red Oil. Thereafter, you shall cast it upon other metal until it is malleable and beautiful of color, and withstands all tests; then you are at the end of the **projection**. When the Stone is completed as is proper—whether it be white or red—as I have taught in the **multiplication** <term>multiplication</term> <note>the process of increasing the Stone's quantity and transmutative power</note>, then its **projection** is infinite, which is unbelievable, for one can hardly reach the end [of its power]. ### On the Philosopher's Stone <note>original: "De lapide Philofophico"</note>

...can barely endure in the innermost furnace / and thus keep it for 5 or 6 days. Afterward, increase the fire / so that the uppermost or outermost furnace hisses / when touched with a wet finger / for 3 days / afterward let it cool by itself / take it out / break open the glass / so you will find a **hard mass** / if you wish to perform a **projection** <term>projection</term> <note>The act of casting a small amount of the Philosopher's Stone onto a molten base metal to transform it into gold or silver.</note> upon Mercury <term>☿</term> <note>original: ☿, the symbol for Quicksilver</note> / then put it into a crucible / then take this mass, pulverize it, and cast it upon [the Mercury] / afterward increase the fire / so that it begins to drive <note>refining the metal by driving off impurities through heat</note> / let it drive for so long / until it prepares itself into a **king** <term>Könige</term> <note>In alchemy, a 'king' (regulus) refers to the pure metallic button found at the bottom of a crucible after smelting.</note> / afterward take it out / let it cool / strike upon it with a hammer / see if it is malleable / then you have accomplished it / and you have good **Gold** <term>sol</term> <note>Latin for 'sun', the standard alchemical name for Gold.</note>.

And note, so long as it is as brittle as glass / then it is still **medicine** <term>medicin</term> <note>The alchemical 'Medicine' or Tincture is the Stone in its potent form before it has been fully 'fixed' into a specific metal.</note> / afterward cast it upon melted Silver <term>☾</term> <note>original: ☾, the symbol for the Moon</note> / until the Silver becomes malleable / and holds together under the hammer / then you have good **Gold** <term>☉</term> <note>original: ☉, the astronomical/alchemical symbol for the Sun</note>. But as long as it is brittle / then you have not yet come to the end of the **projection** / whether it be from the white or red part / afterward you should cast it upon other metal / until it is malleable and beautiful in color / and withstands all tests / then you are at the end of the projection. When the Stone is completed / as is proper / whether it be white or red / as I have taught / in the **multiplication** <note>The process of increasing the power or quantity of the Stone so that a small amount can transform a much larger mass of metal.</note>, then its projection is infinite / which is unbelievable / for one can hardly reach the end of it ; But do nothing else anymore / except to cast on more and more / always upon the
Metal <note>Catchword at the bottom of the page linking to the next leaf.</note>
