## The White and Red Stone First Originated

And it coagulated <note>transformed from a liquid or vapor into a solid mass</note> out of the clean and white **sulfur** <term>sulph.</term> and out of clean **mercury** <term>merc.</term>; thus coagulated, they are fine and pure. For the Stone, as it coagulates, is made from clean and white **sulfur** <term>sulph.</term> and pure **mercury** <term>☿</term>. Thus white **arsenic** <term>arsenicum</term> is pure and subtle <note>refined and penetrating</note>, and it is pure; it is the cleanest substance that has been unified. And with clean red **sulfur** <term>sulph.</term>, when it is mixed with shining **arsenic** <term>arsenico</term>, it is purely clean and much more subtle than the white arsenic.

Example: if one gives a horse 1 **scruple** <term>℈</term> <note>a small unit of weight, approximately 1.3 grams</note>, the horse would die, for the **poison** <term>venenum</term> <note>original Latin: "venenum"</note> would travel to the heart and thereafter to all limbs, so that all the flesh and everything on the horse would become poison, through which it must die. And if dogs or birds eat from this dead horse, they must all die; for if such a **poison** <term>venenum</term> can kill a horse that weighs so many hundreds of pounds, the animals that eat from it must also die. Thus our Stone is made from that same substance, namely from **arsenic** <term>arsenico</term>, be it white or red; and Nature has cooked it thus over a long time, and brought the innermost essence out, and the first **matter** <term>materiam</term> <note>original Latin: "materiam", referring to the "Prima Materia" or the fundamental starting substance of the Great Work</note>...

# On the Philosophical Stone

...a great poison <note>original: *venenum*</note> was turned back into a great medicine; for Gold <note>original: *sol*</note> serves not only for the healing of all illnesses because of its great tempering <note>the balanced state of the four qualities: heat, cold, moisture, and dryness</note>, but also through its mixing.

Our work is made from these two: Gold and Silver <note>original: *sol. & lun.*</note>, as is the custom in the Work. Take Gold and Silver and undo what nature has completed; that is, dissolve what she has matured over many years, and bring it back again into the **first matter** <term>prima materia</term> where it began—where nature first started to work. And it becomes once again an arsenic and a poison <note>original: *venenum*</note>, extracted again with subtle arts and mastery, such that the **matter** <term>materia</term> is more poisonous than it was before nature began to act. If one gave a half-ounce <term>Loth</term> <note>a German unit of weight, approximately 15–17 grams</note> of this **matter** to a horse or an ox, they would die quickly. 

For the **matter** is so subtle, and so thoroughly poisoned and hot, that it is beyond words. Thereafter, sublimate <note>a process of heating a substance until it turns to vapor and then solidifies again</note> the **matter** many times, so that the smoke would kill a man unexpectedly. And if one placed some of this **matter** on a stool, and a man sat upon it for the length of four or five **Lord's Prayers** <note>original: *Pater noster*; a common way to measure short durations of time in the Middle Ages and Renaissance, roughly 2–3 minutes</note>, the **matter** would draw itself upward into the man's body so that he would die quickly. There is also no beast in the world so strong—even if it were a great, poisonous dragon—that if the smoke touched its body, it would die instantly.

### Isaac Hollandus

Therefore, the Ancients compared this **matter** <term>materia</term> to a poisonous dragon, and say that the dragon has eaten its own tail <note>This refers to the Ouroboros, an ancient symbol of a serpent or dragon eating its own tail, representing the cyclical nature of the alchemical work—dissolving and coagulating—and the unity of all things</note>. They also called and compared this **matter** to a snake that impregnates itself, and gave it many other names besides, which would be too long to write down; in truth, they meant only the Stone <note>The Philosopher's Stone</note>.

Thus, one does not find so strong a poison as this **matter** of the Stone, because of its subtlety and the heat it possesses when it is sublimated <note>sublimation is the process of heating a substance until it turns to vapor and then solidifies again, used here to purify the substance to an extreme degree</note>. Then the Master works with his Philosophical Art so that the poison is turned back inward into the innermost part of its body. When this is done, the **matter** has a great medicinal power, which lies hidden in its innermost core; this the Master has brought forth through his art, with the help of God.

When such **matter** of the Stone is tempered <note>tempering refers to the "temperamentum," the perfect balancing of the four primary qualities: heat, cold, moisture, and dryness</note> with such a balance in heat, cold, moisture, and dryness, so that it has become of a heavenly nature, no one can create such a temperament in the **matter** unless it is granted to him by GOD the Lord. And however great a poison it is at first, it becomes just as great a medicine.

When this is so prepared, its innermost is turned out and its outermost is drawn in <note>This paradoxical phrase describes the alchemical "reversal of poles," where the hidden spiritual essence (the soul of the metal) is made manifest and the physical body is spiritualized</note>. Understand this also in relation to the great horse of which I spoke before <note>Referencing the previous page's warning that even a tiny amount of the untempered matter could kill a large animal</note>; such a Stone performs infinite projection <term>projection</term> <note>the final stage of the Great Work, where a tiny amount of the Stone is "projected" onto molten base metal to transform it into gold or silver</note> once it [is brought] into the heavenly nature...

# On the Philosophers' Stone <note>original Latin: "De lapide Philosophico"</note>

just as has now been said of the sun. It <note>the Stone</note> draws out the tinctures <term>Tincture: a potent alchemical liquid or spirit believed to be capable of transmuting or healing matter</term> from the metals and brings them to their utmost potency, so that they become seeds, and from these, the Stone is made. And if God had not created such potency within the metals, even if you threw the Stone upon them a thousand times, no change would occur, for the Stone would not be able to give that which it does not already possess.

I have written that the Stone is formed in many things and is like the ruby, bearing the name of the ruby; it is nothing but the Stone, which is one and the same with crystal glass. The Stone is merely a transmuter <note>original: "Versetzter," one who moves or shifts something from one state to another</note> from evil into good, from the impure into the pure—of everything that is of its own kind—not in all things, but specifically in the metals.

## A Medicine Just and Perfect
->of the second order, to cleanse<-
->copper of all<-
->impurity.<-

Take 20 or 30 pounds of copper, beaten into thin plates <note>original: "lamines"</note>, annealed <note>original: "geglüet," heated until glowing</note> five or six times and quenched in urine, so that they are beautiful and clear; ensure also that they are laminated thin, like pennies, and when they are annealed, that no scales or impurity [remain]

[should be] on it, but rather [they should be] beautifully clear and red; after that, dry them well with a cloth, and subsequently in the sun or by the fire. Then take fine white orpiment <term>original Latin: "album auripigm." (arsenic trisulfide, used here as a whitening agent)</term>, pulverized; grind it on the stone with oil <unclear>very</unclear> small, like Brabant black soap. Then smear the copper plates <term>original Latin: "lamines veneris," referring to copper, the metal associated with the planet Venus</term> and dry them by the fire; and smear them again, and dry it; do this 4 or 5 times. 

Afterward, take common salt <term>original: $\ominus$ (the alchemical symbol for salt)</term>, which is called "pore-salt" <note>likely referring to common salt used to open the "pores" of the metal</note>; crush it small; have a tile <note>or a shallow ceramic vessel</note>, and stratify the contents therein <note>layering the salt and the plates alternatively</note> until the vessel is full. Then seal the vessel with lute <note>a fire-resistant clay used to hermetically seal alchemical vessels</note>; do the same with another vessel, as many as you wish. 

Place it in a calcining furnace <term>original: "calcinir Ofen"</term>; at first with a small fire, then with a larger one, thus following the degrees of heat until they glow through and through. Let them stand thus for 24 hours in the heat; then let it cool, take them out, and "sweeten" the plates <note>in alchemy, to "sweeten" or "edulcorate" means to wash away salts or acids with water</note> well with warm water until they no longer taste of salt <term>symbol: $\ominus$</term>. 

Then dry them well, and crush or grind them small like paint pigment. Afterward, have 2 or 3 large cupels <term>original: "Test," a shallow, porous dish used in assaying or refining</term>; it must be a bowl-like shape. Pour fine water onto it, stir it well, and let the powder sink again; pour that water off again into the first water; do this repeatedly until the water comes off clear. 

After that, dry the powder and mix it with alkaline salt <term>original: "sal alcali Dorace," likely referring to borax or a similar flux</term> and vitriol <term>original: $\oplus$ (the alchemical symbol for vitriol or copperas)</term>; and put it into such a <image-desc>A diagram showing a ribbed, vertically-oriented sack or container, tapered and closed at the top.</image-desc> quite full; sew it together; stick a wooden peg into it in one place; seal the sack all around with good lute-clay, the thickness of 2 fingers

# Concerning the Philosophers’ Stone.

...thick <note>referring to the "lute" or sealant mentioned on the previous page</note>, so that the clay may withstand the fire; let it dry well. Place it in a furnace on two irons; set the crucible underneath, at the spot where you have placed the tap <note>a small opening or spigot in the vessel</note>, and stir the fire on the hearth strongly; it will easily melt and flow into the crucible. Do this until you have brought all your powder into a **body** <term>original: "corpus"</term>. Afterward, weigh it and add to every pound <note>original: "nß," an abbreviation for the Latin *libra*</note> one ounce <term>symbol: ℥</term> of fine **silver** <term>symbol: ☾ (Luna)</term> as it comes from the cupel <note>a refining flask used to purify precious metals</note>. Melt them together in a large melting crucible; pour it into a tin ingot-mold, and thus your **body** is prepared and made "living" <note>an alchemical term for making a substance reactive or spiritually "awake"</note>, and ready for the medicine of the second order and the operation according to the teachings of **Hermes** and **Geber** <note>legendary figures considered the fathers of alchemy</note>.

### An Oil from Strong Water <term>original: "AF" (Aqua Fortis)</term> and Calx of **Lead** <term>symbol: ♄</term>.

Several artists have existed who took Roman vitriol <note>copper sulfate</note>, one pound; calx of **lead** <term>symbol: ♄</term> or lead, and **mercury** <term>symbol: ☿</term> sublimated to red, one pound of each; and one pound of vitriol. (Silver <term>symbol: ☾</term> to the weight of the whole). From this, distill a **strong water** <term>original: "AF" (Aqua Fortis/Nitric Acid)</term> in every manner as described above for the "white stage," and when the water is thus made, then extract the salt from the **Dead Head** <term>original: "Todten-Kopf" (the *caput mortuum*, or the chemically exhausted solid residue left after distillation)</term> as before. Keep the water and salt each separate until you need them. Afterward, take one pound of **mercury** <term>symbol: ☿</term>; sublimate it through three pounds of dry vitriol, and grind it a second time among the dregs <term>original: "feces"</term> on the stone; for the third time, take fresh vitriol.

# Of Isaac Hollandus

Afterward, take the dead-head and the remaining dregs <term>original: feces</term>, grind them to powder, moisten them with vinegar, and extract their salt—as you know how—so that no dregs remain behind. Afterward, increase the fire for 12 hours, then strengthen it for 6 hours, so that it glows mildly; then let it cool. # Of the Philosopher's Stone.

...the salt together with the water distilled over the helm <note>The "helm" or alembic is the cap of a still where vapors condense</note>, as I have ever heard of. 

Believe me in good faith, that I have done wonders with this water which are not to be written here; for I have brought this water myself to a red crystal, which gave off a light by night so that a whole table of people could see to eat their meal by it. Furthermore, take prepared mercury <term>original: merc. præparati</term> from its moisture; for every pound of antimony <term>symbol: ♁</term> take 2 pounds of Roman vitriol <term>term: vitriol. Romani; likely a blue copper sulfate or green iron sulfate used as a catalyst</term> and sublimate it <note>Sublimation is the process of heating a solid until it turns into vapor and then solidifies again, used here for purification</note> through it; rub it back into the dregs <term>original: feces; the solid remains</term>, and for the third time take fresh vitriol and sublimate it again. Thereafter, take 1 lot <term>symbol: nƺ; a historical unit of weight roughly equal to 15 grams</term> of fine gold <term>symbol: ☉</term> beaten thin and cut into rolls; dissolve it in your Aqua Fortis <term>term: AF; nitric acid</term> that you have made, and set it in a basin with sifted ashes, and the basin into a kettle full of water that has been made quite warm. The gold will dissolve from that hour. 

Take also 1 lot of the sublimated mercury; dissolve it also in this water; thereafter put another lot of your mercury into it; let that also dissolve. For it is enough; all 3 lots are inside—dissolved 1 lot of gold and 2 lots of mercury. ## Concerning the Salts of Metals.

Hitherto you have heard, dear sons <note>original: filii; a traditional way for alchemical authors to address their students</note>, how one should manage and act regarding the Great Work <note>The Magnum Opus, or the process of creating the Philosopher's Stone</note> and amalgams, as well as with certain oils. But now you shall hear how salt is made out of metals, which then also yields a perfect elixir <note>A preparation capable of changing base metals into gold or curing all diseases</note> just as well as the oils do. However, its power of projection <note>The ratio by which the elixir can transform base metals</note> is not so high. Nevertheless, it is an easy labor and is accomplished soon in a short time. After this, I will teach you how to make the Stone that I hold for my greatest secret <note>original: secretum</note>.

Know that one may draw salt from all metals, and all salts of the metals are Elixirs, and they are the element of Earth, within which the Fire is hidden. For in the metals there are 4 elements, namely <term>Fire</term> <note>original: Ignis</note>, <term>Air</term> <note>original: Aer</term>, <term>Water</term> <note>original: Aqua</term>, and <term>Earth</term> <note>original: Terra</note>. # Isaac Hollandus

So now take <term>Gold</term> <note>original symbol: ☉</note> and <term>Silver</term> <note>original symbol: ☾</note> and dissolve them in <term>Aqua Fortis</term> <note>original: AF; nitric acid used for dissolving metals</note> and precipitate the <term>powdered metal</term> <note>original: calcem; the ash or powder remaining after a metal has been "burnt" or dissolved and precipitated</note> to the bottom, <term>washed</term> <note>original: abluirt</note> with <term>fresh water</term> <note>original: aqua dulci</note>, and dry it. Thus it is ready to be put into the calcining furnace to be unlocked and opened, so that one may <term>sublimate</term> <note>The process of heating a solid until it becomes a vapor and then solidifies again, used here to extract the essence of the metal</note> the <term>Mercury</term> <note>original symbol: ☿</note> from it. This is not only to be understood regarding <term>Gold</term> and <term>Silver</term>, but also regarding the other metals, such as <term>Lead</term> <note>original symbol: ♄</note>, <term>Tin</term> <note>original symbol: ♃</note>, <term>Copper</term> <note>original symbol: ♀</note>, and <term>Iron</term> <note>original symbol: ♂</note>. Yet those made from <term>Gold</term> and <term>Silver</term> are the best; they also achieve a higher <term>projection</term> <note>The final stage of alchemy where the medicine is cast upon base metals to transmute them into gold or silver</note> and are the easiest to bring into an Oil and <term>Elixir</term>. 

Now take the <term>powdered metal</term> and put it into a glass dish with a wide bottom; lay the <term>powder</term> in there to the thickness of a finger—neither thicker nor thinner. Place it thus in the calcining furnace where one <term>calcines</term> <note>Heats a substance to high temperatures to change its physical state</note> the spirits, or in an <term>athanor</term> <note>A furnace designed to provide a steady, constant heat over long periods</note> or <term>tripod</term>, and give it heat as if one were keeping <term>Lead</term> in a molten state without boiling it away, for twenty-one days, ensuring that the fire does not go out. In this way, the <term>material body</term> <note>original: corpus</note> may be well opened so as to release the <term>Mercury</term> from itself. 

You should know that one must proceed in this way with all metals, whether it be <term>Gold</term> <note>original: sol</note> or <term>Silver</term> <note>original: ☾</note> or another metal, except that <term>Gold</term> must stand for six weeks, because it is a <term>compact</term> and perfect <term>body</term>, which <term>Silver</term> and the other metals are not. Therefore, they do not need to stand so long in the <term>reverberatory furnace</term> <note>original: Reverber; a furnace that reflects the heat onto the material from the roof</note>.

# On the Philosophical Stone

Make a fire beneath it, at first small, then increased degree by degree until it begins to glow. Then keep it glowing so for 8 or 10 days; however, do not make the fire too strong, so that it does not melt <note>original: "flüfse," literally "flow"; the author warns against liquefying the solid metal powder into a mass, which would prevent sublimation</note>. You will find the <term>Mercury</term> <note>original: Merc:</note> sublimated there, as white as snow; save it.

You will find the <term>Salt</term> or <term>Earth</term> at the bottom of the glass in the form of a white-gray powder, and it has swollen up like a sponge because the <term>Mercury</term> <note>original symbol: ☿</note> has been drawn from it. Now take the <term>Salt</term> or <term>Earth</term> out of the glass, put it into a stone jar, and pour <term>distilled wine vinegar</term> over it. Place it in a boiling <term>water bath</term> <note>original: balneum</note> and let it stand for 4 or 6 days, often stirred and well covered. Then let it settle and cool; pour off the liquid and keep it. ## On the Oils from the Metals.

Now let us proceed to the projection: take a large quantity of **Roman Vitriol** <note>original: "vitriol. Let it evaporate until a skin forms on top; then let it cool, and the vitriol will "shoot" <note>An alchemical term for crystallization</note> a beautiful green, which is the best color in its <term>simple</term> state.

Then dissolve it again, and it will let more "earth" <note>Impurities or solid matter</note> fall to the bottom; pour it off, let it evaporate, and let it crystallize again as mentioned before. <margin>I iii</margin>

Then dissolve and granulate it again; do this so often until it leaves no <term>dregs</term> <note>original: "feces"; the solid waste or sediment that remains after a substance is dissolved or purified</note> behind. Keep this until you have need of it.

Now, someone might ask: since the vitriol is dissolved, why do you not let it evaporate completely, but instead it must "shoot" [crystallize] in this way, which takes much time before one brings it into a white powder? Know then that the vitriol has within it a subtle <term>spirit</term> <note>original: "Spiritum"; the volatile, life-giving essence of a substance</note>, just as is described in the *Vegetable* [treatise] <note>Likely referring to Hollandus’s own work *Opus Vegetabile*</note> concerning the subtle spirits that reside on the outside of all herbs. That is the "Greenness" of all things that are green on the outside; for that is the flower of their true essence. If you lose the Greenness, I tell you truly: you are robbed of the essence, and whatever further work you do with it is all lost, for it has lost much: its soul, life, and essence. It is a dead <term>body</term> <note>original: "corpus"; the physical matter remaining after the spiritual essence has been removed or destroyed</note>, whether it be herbs, leaves, spirits, or roots, and everything that is green on the outside. Therefore, see to it that you guard it well, for it is so subtle that you may lose it invisibly, as is contained in the *Vegetable* [treatise], where it is taught how one [extracts] the Greenness from all herbs, leaves, and roots, from everything that is green on the outside...

# Concerning the Philosophers’ Stone.

Put a large <term>still-head</term> <note>original: *Helm*; the glass or ceramic cap of a distilling apparatus</note> on, and set it in sifted ash, and make a small fire beneath it, and distill off its strong water. Then strengthen the fire so that the <term>mercury</term> <note>original: *merc.*</note> distills over completely. Let it cool and take the mercury out; put it into a glass with a long neck, just as you did with the <term>vitriol</term> <note>original: *vitriol.*; a sulfate mineral used as a base for many alchemical acids</note>. Set it in ash and make a <term>lamp-fire</term> <note>a low, constant heat source used for gentle incubation</note> beneath it, and let it stand until it becomes perfectly red.

As was taught regarding the vitriol, <term>dissolve</term> <note>original: *solvirs*</note> and <term>coagulate</term> <note>original: *coagulirs*</note> it. When it is clean, distill it into a red oil, just as was said of the vitriol. However, the mercury passes over entirely into an oil and leaves no <term>dregs</term> <note>original: *feces*; the solid waste or residue left after distillation</note> behind. One proceeds with all metals in no other way than has just been said of mercury.

### Isaac Hollandus

# On the Philosophical Stone

Calcine the glass <note>In this context, "glass" often refers to a vitrified or transparent state of a mineral, specifically "Vitriol-glass."</note> until it reaches its perfect redness, and afterward dissolve it in vinegar and cast away its **dregs** <term>feces</term> <note>original: *feces*; the solid impurities or sediment that settle out of a liquid</note> which it holds within its innermost redness, and which at first would not leave it until its inner nature was brought out. 

For you should know that every thing contains two kinds of dregs: first in its coarseness, when it was still raw; as when you first dissolved and **coagulated** <note>the process of thickening a liquid into a solid</note> **vitriol** <note>original: *vitriol.*; a sulfate mineral used as a primary material in alchemy</note>; at that time it was still green and without dregs. But now that it has become red and is dissolved again, it leaves behind other dregs which were not seen before. 

Now one might ask: how is it that the innermost part will not let go of its dregs, even though the entire material appears to be a clear water? You should understand it thus: when the material is dissolved, the material stands fully exposed and encloses the heat so that the heat cannot leave it. But when the heat has been brought out <note>referring to the "inner heat" or sulfurous nature being externalized through calcination</note> and is then dissolved, it opens itself and lets its dregs go, because it is no longer constrained by the cold. 

Therefore, one must **rectify** <note>to purify a substance through repeated distillation or chemical processing</note> twice, if one wishes to make a true **elixir** or **quintessence** <term>quint. essentiam</term> <note>original: *quint. essentiam*; the "fifth essence" or the most purified, potent form of a substance</note>, no matter what the material may be—whether in **spirits** <term>Spiritibus</term> <note>original: *Spiritibus*; volatile substances or distilled essences</note>, in herbs, in roots, sugar, honey, and the like. And I charge you by my God, that never has such a **secret** <note>original: *secretum*</note> been revealed, nor has it ever been set down in any writing, except by me alone, by my own hand.

Now further, you have also heard how one

## Now we wish to say, dear children, how we may multiply the Oil of the Philosophers.

# Isaac Hollandus

Read this often and keep it well in your memory, for it is necessary for you above all other things, because in this lies hidden the foundation of all arts.

If you wish, however, to draw a simple oil from the metals, as has been taught regarding **vitriol** <note>a sulphate mineral, often of iron or copper, prized for its ability to produce strong acids</note>, you must dissolve your metal in <term>aqua fortis</term> <note>original: *aq.f.*; literally "strong water," referring to nitric acid</note> and precipitate it, sweeten <note>to wash away the salts or acids until the water runs neutral</note>, dry, or **coagulate** it, and place it in a vial, set it in the ashes <note>an ash-bath used for gentle, even heating in a furnace</note>, and proceed with the governance of the fire as was taught regarding vitriol, until it becomes entirely red. This is a perfect <term>oil of the philosophers</term> <note>original: *oleum Philosophorum*; a refined liquid state of a metal believed to have transformative properties</note>, but its **projection** <note>the final stage of the alchemical process where the "medicine" is thrown upon base metals to transmute them</note> is not as high as the oil which came from the first salt.

You should **multiply** <note>to increase the quantity or potency of the alchemical substance</note> all <term>metallic oils</term> <note>original: *olea metallorum*</note> with the <term>oil of vitriol</term> <note>original: *oleo vitrioli*; concentrated sulfuric acid</note>, as has been taught; and one can easily make oil of <term>Lead</term> <note>original: the symbol ♄, representing Saturn or Lead</note> in the same manner, and its projection is very high. Thank **GOD** and do not use His gifts wickedly, for the sake of your soul’s salvation.

# Isaac Hollandus

...works nothing; thus all things that are purified according to the course of Nature transform themselves first into earth. And once a thing is turned into earth, it is open and unlocked, allowing each element to be extracted, as is demonstrated in the **Vegetable** [treatise] <note>Hollandus often refers to his work on "Vegetables" (plants), implying that the laws of nature are consistent across the plant and mineral kingdoms</note>. If a thing is to be transformed from the first degree into another, it is necessary that it be changed into another nature, unless it has first become earth because of the <term>dregs</term> <note>original: *fecum*; the solid impurities or sediment within a substance</note> it contains within itself.

Take an example from lead: when it is stirred over the fire, it must first turn into earth or ash before the potters can use it for glazing. For lead cannot become glass unless it first becomes earth, the cause being the <term>dregs</term> <note>original: *feces*</note> it contains. And you can make silver <note>original symbol: ☾ (the Moon), representing silver</note> from it, but it must first become glass, as is taught in many places. One might well turn **Saturn** <note>the alchemical name for lead</note> into silver by other means, if one were to gently remove its dregs, as is sufficiently taught in its proper place. For in its innermost self, lead is silver, and it lacks nothing else than for its <term>dregs</term> and its earthy **sulphur** <note>in alchemy, sulphur represents the "soul" or combustible principle of a metal; "earthy sulphur" here refers to the impure, non-metallic part</note> to be separated from it; then it is as good silver as that which Nature transforms daily, as can be seen.

Look at the old chapels which were roofed with lead in ancient times and have lain there for fully 600 years, so that the lead has been entirely converted into earth, and lies white upon the metal <note>This refers to "white lead" or basic lead carbonate, the white crust that forms on lead through natural weathering</note>, as is found daily on old lead roofs.

While the previous page focused on the natural purification of water, oils, and wine over time, this page shifts to the active alchemical use of fire to purify metals and create imitation gemstones.</meta>

# On the Philosopher’s Stone

Things / which occur through fire / concerning this **rectification** <term>rectification; the process of refining or purifying a substance, usually through repeated distillation or heat</term> we wish to report something / as much as is necessary / you will also find a clearer explanation in the **Vegetable Work** <note>original: *Vegetabili*; likely referring to Hollandus’s treatise *De Vegetabilibus*</note> / regarding the imitation of stones and the making of precious **amauzes** <term>amauſen; colored glass pastes used to create counterfeit or "imitation" gemstones</term>.

Therefore, know / that one may cleanse all metals of their **dregs** <note>original Latin: *fecibus*; the impure sediment or waste matter</note> with the fire / and the **Elements** remain perfectly together without separation / and thereafter they may be **fixed** <term>figiren; to make a volatile substance stable so that it does not evaporate or change when exposed to heat</term> / and all of this shall happen with the fire / and this occurs in many different manners / **Gold** <note>symbol: ☉</note> must be **calcined** <term>calcined; reduced to a powder by roasting or burning</term> differently than **Silver** <note>symbol: ☽</note> / and **Silver** <note>symbol: ☽</note> must be burned differently than **Lead** <note>symbol: Saturn</note> / and **Copper** <note>symbol: Venus</note> must be burned differently than **Iron** <note>symbol: ♂</note>. My child should know / that two kinds of burning may occur in the metals / one **by cementation** <note>original Latin: *per cementationem*; a dry refining process where metal is heated in contact with powdered chemical agents</note> / of which more will follow hereafter in its own treatise / and a cementation occurs with salt / and with mineral spirits / without the transformation of the metals into glass / and yet one occurs differently than the other.

All metals are transformed into glass without any additives / except for **Gold** <note>symbol: ☉</note> alone / which will not allow itself to be transformed into any glass / even if one burns it until the Day of Judgment / one must add something to it / which the others do not require / for **Gold** <note>symbol: ☉</note> is pure in its nature / yet it has a few **dregs** <note>original: *feces*</note> about it / but not so many / that they might cover the **body** <note>original Latin: *corpus*; the physical substance of the metal</note> / and no matter how violently one burns it / the dregs still do not turn to glass / it would sooner burn away entirely and come to nothing / before it [turns to glass].

<meta>catchword: ſe/das (it/the)</meta>

<summary>This page explains the "rectification" of metals using fire, distinguishing between the process of cementation and vitrification. It notes that while most metals can be turned into glass, gold is uniquely pure and resists vitrification even under extreme heat.</summary>

# Isaac Hollandus

...themselves; but the other metals do not do that / <unclear>b</unclear> they would naturally become glass. Now one might ask / if a thing has become glass / has it then been brought to its **utmost extreme**? <note>Referring to the "final state" or the end of a substance's evolutionary potential in the physical world.</note> For if a thing has become glass / it can be reduced to no other material / because glass is the outermost state of all things. And on the **Last Day** <note>A reference to the Day of Judgment; alchemists often believed the world would be purified by fire into a crystalline or glass-like state.</note> everything that is under the firmament shall be turned into glass / by the decree of God. Therefore it is clear / that glass is the **ultimate matter of all things** <note>original Latin: *ultima materia omnium rerum*</note> / and cannot be brought to any other thing through the **arts** <note>Meaning the art of alchemy.</note> / except to a burnt material / or to the **dregs** <note>original Latin: *fecibus*; the waste or sediment remaining after a process.</note>.

For I have often seen / that through long and strong burning, glass was brought to black **slag** / like iron slag / and through even longer burning / it became white ashes / light as dust / so that it blew away / and thus the glass came to nothing. Thus it appears that glass is the **ultimate matter** <note>original Latin: *ultima materia*</note> of all things / except for metallic things / and this is the reason: in all things that one may burn / the **oil** <term>oil; in alchemy, this often refers to the "soul" or the flammable, oily principle (Sulfur) within a substance</term> and natural moisture separate from their **earth** <term>earth; the solid, physical residue or "body" (Salt) of a substance</term> through strong burning. 

But from metals and mineral things / the oil does not separate from the earth in the fire / but instead remains constant in the fire with the earth at all times. For if the oil departed from the earth / then no **elixir** <term>elixir; a perfected alchemical substance capable of transmuting metals or healing the body</term> could be made from it / and the **salts of metals** <note>original Latin: *salia metallorum*</note> must then be fusible <note>able to be melted</note> / how could they then...

<summary>Hollandus explains that glass is the final, irreversible state of most matter, representing the end of its physical transformation, except in metals where the "oil" and "earth" remain inseparable.</summary>

# On the Philosophical Stone

...give **ingress** <term>ingress: the ability of the alchemical elixir to penetrate and "enter" into the body of a base metal to transform it</term> / and tinctures. # Of Isaac Hollandus

...[earth] and fire are two **fixed elements** <term>fixed elements: substances that resist the power of fire and do not easily vaporize</term>, therefore they keep within them as much **air** as is necessary for them to give an **ingress** <term>ingress: the ability of an alchemical substance to penetrate and transform a base metal</term>.

How does it happen then, someone might ask, that all **metals** may become **glass**, except for **Gold** <note>represented by the symbol ☉</note> alone? Is this the reason: all metals are impure and full of **dregs** <note>original Latin: *fecum*; refers to the "earthy" impurities in base metals</note>, therefore one **calcines** <term>calcine: to reduce a metal to a powder (calx) through prolonged heating</term> them easily; for all parts of the metals are full of **sulfurous dregs**, which they acquired in the **mine** <note>The "mine" refers to the natural subterranean environment where the metal grew.</note>. When they then come into a powerful fire, they calcine easily, and the moisture of the fatty sulfurous dregs burns away, and the dregs become so dry that the entire metal turns into **earth** and **lime** <note>original Latin: *calcem*</note>. And if they did not become lime, one could never turn them into glass, for it must be lime.

One cannot bring any thing from one nature into another except through a **medium**. For previously it was metal, and now it is glass—these are indeed two different natures. If one is to accomplish this, it must happen through a medium, which is the **earth** or **lime** of the metal, which is then turned into glass through strong and long burning, as is taught regarding the **imitation** <note>original: *Conterfäihung*</note> of **precious stones**. And when they are now imitated, they are ten times more beautiful and clearer than **oriental** <note>original: *trientische (orientische)*; "Oriental" stones (from the East) were considered the highest quality natural gems.</note> ones, and much lovelier to look upon.

Then look at the **glass pastes** <term>original: *Amausen*; synthetic glass used to imitate gemstones</term> with which one **enamels** <note>original: *ameliret*</note>, how lovely green, blue, yellow, red, and white they are; and one is half as beautiful again as the

<summary>This page explains that base metals can be transformed into glass because their sulfurous impurities allow them to be calcined into "lime," whereas gold's purity prevents this. The author also notes that synthetic gems made from these metallic glasses can surpass natural oriental stones in beauty.</summary>

# Of the Philosopher’s Stone.

...others are more precious / yet they are only made of metal / how does it happen then that one is more beautiful and precious than the other / and also more fluid? You should know / once the metal is **calcined** <term>calcined: reduced to a powder through heat</term> / and thereafter placed in the vaulted furnace or **reverberatory furnace** <term>reverber: a furnace where the flame or heat is reflected onto the material rather than touching it directly</term> / it must have great heat / before it will become a beautiful, clear, and transparent glass / and before the **dregs** <note>original Latin: *feces*</note> clear away / and the better it clears / the more beautiful and fluid it becomes / and you should know that the glass made in this way may be compared to a **glorious body** <note>original Latin: *glorioso corpori*</note>, whereas before it was a black, impure **body** <note>original Latin: *corpus*</note> / and within this **body** is hidden the noble **Quintessence** <term>quinta Essentia: the purest part of a substance, believed by alchemists to be its most potent medical and spiritual core</term> of the metal / which is incombustible / and shines with its precious colors until the Last Day / and thus its soul is in a **clarified** <note>original: *clarificirtem*</note> **body** just like a light in a crystal lantern / and one soul shines much clearer than the other; Thus you should know / that the **Quintessence** or the incombustible oil of the metal / together with its clear salt, shines and gleams out of the **metallic glass** <note>original Latin: *vitro metallico*</note>, like a candle out of the lantern / and the more precious one is / the more readily it flows. Now furthermore you should understand / once the metal is **calcined** / and the master wants to make a green **imitation gem** <term>Amauſen: glass or enamel pastes used to mimic precious stones like emeralds</term> from it / then he must burn it from **Copper** <note>symbolized in text by ♀</note> / and if he wishes to have a beautiful green surpassing all other greens / in color and clarity / then he must grind the lime upon a marble stone / with salt-water until it is impalpable <note>meaning ground so finely it cannot be felt between the fingers</note> / and afterwards washed again / until the water is clear and beautiful

Therefore one burns it in many works / where one reverberates it <term>reverberiret: heating a substance in a furnace where the flame is reflected back onto the material</term> / so that one gives it no greater heat / than that it glows / for if one makes the fire stronger / it turns itself into **glass**. ### CEMENTATION <term>cementatio: a process of heating a metal with a powdered reagent, such as salt, to purify it or change its properties</term>

Furthermore know / that there is yet another [type of] burning / and it is called **cementation**, which is a **calcination** <term>calcinatio: the reduction of a metal to a powder by heat</term> / yet there is a difference / for in common calcination one takes **prepared rock salt** <note>original: *sal gemmæ præparatæ*</note> and **metal plates** <note>original: *laminas Metallorum*</note> / and layers them <note>original: *stratificirts*</note> until the crucible is full / then seals the crucible well with **lute** <term>verlutirt: sealed with airtight paste or clay</term> / and places it into the fire / and gives it a natural glow / if it is **Copper** <note>symbol: ♀</note> [for] 2 days / if it is **Silver** <note>original: *lun.* (Luna)</note> [for] 24 hours / if it is **Tin** <note>symbol: ♃</note> [for] 8 days / if it is **Lead** <note>symbol: ♄</note> [for] 2 days / and so on / that is the manner of calcining.

But the **method of cementation** <note>original: *modus cementationis*</note> happens similarly to the above / only the difference is / that one comes to its aid / with some mi- <note>catchword: nera-; likely "minerals"</note>

## On the Philosophers' Stone.

Now I wish to teach you a work / that I have performed with my own hands / and it is an easy work to carry out / without much effort or worry / and it is one of my **secrets** <note>original: *secreten*</note>. # Isaac Hollandus

### Fixation of the Moon. <note>original: *Lunæ Fixatio*; refers to the alchemical process of making silver stable in fire or preparing it for transformation.</note>

First, you should take fine **Silver** <note>symbol: ☾; original: *luna*</note> from the cupel <term>capellen: a shallow, porous furnace vessel used in the refining of precious metals</term> / and fine **Gold** <note>symbol: ☉; original: *sol*</note> according to your pleasure / beat them thin into plates as thick as a coin <note>original: *orint*; likely referring to an "Ort," a small German coin</note> / and half a hand wide / and for the Silver, take prepared **rock salt** <note>original: *sal gemmæ*</note> and **orpiment** <term>auripigm.: arsenic trisulfide, used for its volatile nature and yellow color</term> sublimated to whiteness / with one [part] **vinegar** <note>original: †; this symbol often indicates *acetum* (vinegar) or vitriol in this context</note> and **calamine stone** <term>lap. calammari: a zinc ore used in the production of brass</term> in equal amounts : And through 3 pounds of this sublimated [mixture], sublimate 1 pound of orpiment 3 or 4 times / each time rubbing it back into the **dregs** <note>original: *feces*; the solid residue remaining after sublimation</note> / then the orpiment is ready.

Now take for 1 pound of prepared salt 2 drachms <note>symbol: ʒ</note> of the prepared orpiment and 2 drachms of white **calcined tartar** <term>tartarum: the potassium salt crust found in wine barrels, purified by high heat</term>, which is very clear and transparent / and mix it well together. With this powder, make **layer upon layer** <note>original: *stratum super stratum*</note> in a good tile-vessel <note>original: *Ziegel*; likely a ceramic crucible or luted box</note> with the plates of Silver / as you know, [each layer] a finger thick / seal it well with luting clay / and set it to **cement** <note>the process of heating a metal in contact with powdered reagents to change its properties</note> in a moderate heat / so that the Silver does not melt. Let it stand thus for 36 hours / then let it cool / and break open the vessel / thus the Silver is **calcined** <note>reduced to a powder or brittle state by heat</note>, and it is black and brittle as glass.

Then take clean water / and wash the Silver / to remove the blackness and saltiness / let it dry / and crush it in a mortar / afterwards rub it very well with distilled water / then take a glass bowl / and put in the rubbed Silver and wash it well with clean water / and let it settle a little / or pour it off into another glass / onto the powder pour fresh water again / stir it and wash it again for so long until the water runs off as clear as you [poured it]

<summary>This page provides a practical recipe for the calcination and fixation of silver, using a mixture of orpiment, rock salt, and tartar. ### On the Philosopher’s Stone
<note>original: "De lapide Philoſophico"</note>

Know that if the crystals do not reveal themselves, the matter has become <term>fixed: rendered stable and resistant to evaporation by heat</term> by the other heat. Do not let this concern you, for it is better that everything is fixed with low heat than with high heat. For if you give it heat from the start like the sun shining in mid-summer, it will "shoot" or grow crystals as long and wide as a hand; but that would not be good.

It is better that the <term>Stone: the developing substance of the Great Work</term> be cooked with low heat, so that no crystal points or small stones grow out of it. If too many grow, and you then give it a little too much heat, the <term>spirit: the volatile, gaseous essence of the matter</term> <note>original: "Spiritus"</note> rises up in the form of a white smoke and clings to the top of the glass. If this happens, you must open the glass and scrape the <term>matter: the physical substance being transformed</term> <note>original: "materia"</note> back down. 

Take care that you do not give it too much heat so that it rises, lest your matter become yellow or red like a pebble; for then it would be ruined, and you would have to start over again from the beginning. Therefore, see that you do not make your fire too strong, so that you do not obtain the "Yellow" or "Red" states before the "White." It is better that the fire be too small than too large.

When it is now perfectly white and no crystal points shoot out, the matter is fixed. Then divide the matter into two parts: one half for the "White" <note>the *Albedo* or white stage, used to create an elixir that transmutes base metals into silver</note> and the other for the "Red" <note>the *Rubedo* or red stage, the final perfection used to create gold</note>. # On the Philosophers' Stone <note>original Latin: "De lapide Philosophico"</note>

...know that if the crystals do not reveal themselves, it has become **fixed** <note>reached a stable, non-volatile state where it no longer evaporates</note> through the other heat. Do not let this worry you, for it is better that everything be fixed with a small heat than with a great one. For if you first give it heat like the sun shining in mid-summer, it will shoot crystals as long and as wide as a hand's breadth; but that would not be good.

It is better that the **stone** <note>the alchemical substance in development</note> be cooked with a small heat, so that no crystal points or small stones grow out of it. If you allow too many to grow, and you then give it a little too much heat, the <term>spirit: the volatile or vaporous part of the substance</term> <note>original: "Spiritus"</note> will rise up in the form of a white smoke and cling to the top of the glass. And if this happens, you must open the glass and scrape the <term>matter: the physical substance being transformed</term> <note>original: "materiam"</note> back down again. Guard yourself against giving it too much heat so that it rises, lest your matter become yellow or red like a flint stone; for then it would be spoiled, and you would have to begin anew. Therefore, see to it that you do not make your fire too strong, so that you do not obtain the **Yellow** or **Red** before the **White**; and it is better for the fire to be too small than too large.

When it is now perfectly **white**, and no crystalline points shoot out, then the matter is fixed. Then divide the matter into two parts: one half for the **White** <note>the stage of the 'White Work' or *albedo*, which yields the power to transmute metals into silver</note>, and the other half for the **Red** <note>the 'Red Work' or *rubedo*, the final stage for transmuting metals into gold</note>. Now take the half part that you wish to bring to the Red, set it in the ashes <note>the ash-bath for indirect, even heating</note>, and bring it to redness; the other half for the

### Extraction of Living Corporal Mercury <note>original Latin: "Extractio Merc. vivi corporalis"</note>

Now I wish to teach you how to make the Paradise Water out of **Silver** <note>original symbol: ☽</note>, and you should know that it is extracted in two ways. The first method has already been explained in the Work, but this one is made in a different manner, for this Silver must pass over "living." It proceeds almost in the same way, except that for the **Salmiac** <note>original symbol: ✳; ammonium chloride, used as a flux in alchemy</note> one takes clear, transparent, calcined Salmiac, in the same weight as the Salmiac. And when you have ground the **lime of Silver** <note>original: "calcem ☽"; silver that has been reduced to a powder through heat or acid</note> with the **Salt** <note>original symbol: ✕</note>, you do not need to place the matter in the calcining furnace for very long to open it. Instead, put it into a glass and pour the vinegar upon it, and distill the vinegar off as was taught before. Thus the **Mercury** <note>original: "Mercurius"</note> passes over the **alembic** <note>original: "alambicum"; a distilling apparatus consisting of two vessels connected by a tube</note> drop by drop through the power of nature which the Salt carries with it. Put this **Venus** <note>original symbol: ♀, usually representing Copper; here likely referring to the metallic distillate or the vessel's specific state</note> into a glass flask and draw off its <term>phlegma: the watery, tasteless, or inactive part of a distilled liquid</term>, as taught before.

### INCERATION <note>original Latin: "Inceratio"; the process of softening a dry substance to a wax-like consistency</note>

Afterward, take it out and pour nine parts of Mercury onto one part of the **White Stone**. Seal it well and place it again in the ashes <note>an ash-bath for even, gentle heating</note> next to the other glass in which the **Red Stone** is kept, and give it a slightly hotter fire; for

# On the Philosophers' Stone <note>original Latin: "De lapide Philosophico"</note>

The stone may be finished / yet if it cannot become red / then you must pursue the fire from degree to degree / until it gains other colors / and the Stone becomes somewhat darker than the yellow / always strengthening the fire from 6 days / to 5 days <note>implying a gradual increase in intensity over these intervals</note> / until the **material** becomes perfectly red / until it is like a **ruby** / and shines in the glass like **fish-eyes** / for then you cannot make the fire too hot / even if it were to glow entirely / because once the signs are seen / the Stone is perfected and fixed / and I tell you this so that you shall keep measure in the fire / for if you give it too hot a fire / so that the redness comes before the yellow / then you must start anew / or if you obtain the whiteness before the blackness / it is the same case.

Therefore it is always better to have too small / rather than too great a fire / and proceed only from degree to degree / as the colors change / and heighten / so one should strengthen the fire / and this is not only in this work / but in everything / for the right **regulation of the fire** / is the other masterpiece in our art / and unless the proportion is well observed / then all is lost.

Now onward / when the material is fixed / and as red as a ruby / then take it out / and incerate it <term>Inceration</term> <note>The alchemical process of adding liquid to a dry substance to soften it to a wax-like consistency</note> with beautiful **Paradise Water** <note>A highly purified solvent or quintessence</note> / that has been drawn from **Gold** <term>☉</term> <note>original symbol: Sol/Sun, representing gold</note> / (just as was said of the silver <note>original: "luna"</note>) / and pour 9 parts of Paradise Water onto 1 part of the red Stone / and seal the glass at the top / with

# Isaac Hollandus <note>Latin: Isaaci Hollandi</note>

...with strong **Lute** <note>original: "Luto"; a sealant made of clay and binders used to airtight vessels</note> / and set it back into the furnace in the ashes / and give it fire / as if you were keeping [it] molten without agitation / until it has entirely drunk in the **Paradise Water** / so it has become a gray powder / yet more black than gray / then keep the fire in the same heat / until it becomes truly white / then increase your fire noticeably / until you see / that the **material** begins to turn yellow / then increase the fire a little more / until you see / that it is dark yellow / from degree to degree / and when your powder is all brown / like **saffron** / when it can become no yellower / then make your fire noticeably stronger / until you see the red color emerge / so continue with the fire / until it becomes perfectly red / like a **ruby** / then rejoice / for the **Stone for the Red** is completed / so let it cool.
